Status values: ⬜ Not started · 🔵 In progress · 🟡 Blocked · 🟠 Awaiting human review · ✅ Complete (all acceptance criteria + DoD evidenced below)

## Update protocol (mandatory for every agent session)

1. **On session start:** read this file top to bottom. Resume from the first non-✅ phase. Never re-do ✅ work; never skip a 🟡 blocker without resolving it.
2. **On phase start:** set status 🔵 with date; copy that phase's *Acceptance criteria* checklist verbatim into the phase section below.
3. **During work:** append Session log entries (date, what was done, key commands/results). Record every deviation or decision in the Decisions log — a decision without a written rationale is a bug.
4. **On acceptance check:** tick each criterion only with one line of evidence (test name + count, command output summary, or e2e spec name). Unproven ticks are forbidden.
5. **On phase completion:** verify the Definition of Done ([01-architecture.md](01-architecture.md) §20), set ✅ with date, update the status table, commit.
6. **On blocker:** set 🟡, fill a Blockers entry (symptom, what was tried, smallest human decision needed), STOP work on that phase.
7. **Human review items:** add to the Human review queue; these do not block progress unless marked GATE.

### Phase 11 — AdSense review readiness · 🔵 In progress (2026-08-24)

Plan: [phase-11-adsense-review.md](phase-11-adsense-review.md). CLI handoff: **done** — [2026-08-24-handoff-to-cli-phase-11-adsense-review.md](../docs/archived/2026-08-24-handoff-to-cli-phase-11-adsense-review.md) (revision 2, written 2026-08-21 after Phase 12), executed end to end and archived with an Executed banner.

**This phase is split, and the split is the point.** The work order has five
workstreams and **three of them cannot start without a domain the owner has not
bought yet**: 11A (the cutover), 11D (the indexing bake) and 11E (the
application loop). This branch does the other two in full — 11B (contact and
identity) and 11C (the editorial layer) — plus the mechanical quality floors,
the tool-page top-up, and 11A reduced to a one-line change for the day the
domain exists. **The status stays 🔵 and does not go ✅**: the phase's terminal
criterion is an external one (Google shows the domain approved), and six of the
eleven criteria below are owner or calendar work.

What this branch can and cannot tick, stated once so the list below is
readable: it can tick everything that is code, copy, contract or docs
(`/contact`, the About paragraph, the twelve guides, the floors, the four
contract sites, §21.6, the README row). It cannot tick anything requiring the
domain (the 301s, the canonical sweep on the apex, the `support@<domain>` mail
test), anything requiring a Google property (Search Console, the submission
gate, the application), or anything requiring a human (the P4 legal review, the
owner's skim of the twelve guides).

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-11-adsense-review.md, ticked only with evidence):

- [ ] Old host and `www` 301 to the apex; deploys still pass healthcheck; canonicals, `og:url`, JSON-LD, sitemap and robots all name the apex; `seo-artifacts.spec.ts` green on regenerated artifacts; `FRONTEND_BASE_URL`/`API_BASE_URL` mint links on the new origin. → **owner-gated: the domain does not exist.** Parameterised so the cutover is one commit: `grep -rn zenpdf.up.railway.app frontend/src frontend/tools` returns **exactly one hit**, `core/site.ts:23`, and everything else derives from it. The nginx redirect block is written, commented out, and placed after the app block, which now carries `listen 80 default_server;` explicitly. Both orderings were **run against a real nginx**: correct order → apex `200`, healthcheck `200`, old host and `www` `301` to the apex; wrong order → the apex `301`s to itself and **the Railway healthcheck `301`s, which fails every deploy**. Runbook: `docs/ops/domain-cutover.md`.
- [ ] Search Console: domain property verified, sitemap submitted and read. → **owner-gated**: needs the domain and a Google property. Nothing has been submitted.
- [~] `/contact` live, prerendered, in the sitemap, linked from the site footer everywhere; a real mail to `support@<domain>` arrived in the owner's inbox. → **the page half is done; the mailbox half is owner-gated.** `/contact` renders on the legal reading column with a `mailto:` and **zero** `form`/`input`/`textarea`/submit elements (`legal-page.spec.ts`, `phase-11.spec.ts`), is prerendered (`dist/contact/index.html`, `verify-prerender` asserts it), is in the sitemap, and is reachable from the site footer on landing, a tool page and a legal page (clicked, not assumed). The address is `yuval3000+support@gmail.com` — a placeholder the owner chose when asked, because `support@<domain>` cannot exist yet; the mail test is the criterion that stays open.
- [x] About page answers "who runs this" and links `/contact`. → a "Who runs this" section naming no individual — independent product, no support department, a real address — with `data-test="about-identity"` and a link to `/contact`. `legal-page.spec.ts` *"answers 'who runs this' and links /contact"*; `phase-11.spec.ts` clicks the link through to `/contact`.
- [x] 12 guides + `/guides` index live, prerendered, in the sitemap, each passing the mechanical floor and the extended `verify:prerender`. → **12 guides, 10,560 words of body prose, 792–1,123 each** against a 700-word floor; `guide-pages.spec.ts` holds words, sections, unique title/meta/H1, resolving `relatedTools`, slug uniqueness across tools **and** guides, well-formed dates, a literal route and a prerender entry per slug, and that no `guides/:slug` route exists. `ng build` → **Prerendered 43 static routes** (29 + `/contact` + `/guides` + 12, checked against the route files). `verify:prerender` asserts each guide's own `<title>`, H1, canonical, `Article` JSON-LD, **that the prose is in the served HTML**, and that every guide is linked from `/guides`. Sitemap: **43 `<loc>`s**, guides at priority 0.6.
- [x] All 24 tool pages meet the intro/FAQ floors (4 intros topped up, 15 fourth-FAQs written). → **re-measured first; the work order's 2026-08-10 figures were stale.** Actual on 2026-08-24: **3** intros under 250 (`jpg-to-pdf` 218, `add-page-numbers` 223, `pdf-to-jpg` 247) and **14** pages with 3 FAQs — `extract-pdf-pages` had been topped up by the 08-18 options-row work. All now clear: intros **254–411 words**, every page ≥ 4 FAQs. `tool-pages.spec.ts` holds 250/4. A diff of the whole table against `main` shows every pinned field identical and every pre-existing paragraph and FAQ present verbatim, **with one deliberate exception**: the merge-pdf bookmark paragraph, which was factually wrong (see the Decisions log).
- [x] Design contract amended at all four sites (grounding, §3, §4, §10) in the same changes as the UI work; `01-architecture.md` §21.6 amended; README index row added; both themes verified on every new/changed surface. → contract amended at the grounding list, §3 Footer (five links → seven, with the ceremony footer and the legal crosslink row explicitly excluded), §4 (two new screen entries), §10 (invariants), plus the §11 Amendment log row — landed **before** any UI, in `cb8580e`. §21.6 gains the prerendered surface as a table of four kinds with priorities and where each is generated from. README status line and index row updated. Both themes verified in a real browser at 1280 px and a true 390 px: evidence and measurements in `docs/reviews/evidence/phase-11/`.
- [ ] Privacy policy carries the AdSense-required advertising-cookie disclosure (checked against answer/1348695 as part of the P4 review). → **owner-gated (the existing P4 GATE)**: this is part of the human legal review, and no wording was changed here. The current copy ("Advertising cookies, set by Google, *only* after you allow them") is untouched by this branch.
- [ ] Submission gate met (11D — indexing target or the 4-week timebox). → **calendar work**, and it cannot start before the domain.
- [ ] Owner has skim-read all guides before submission. → **owner-gated, and the one that matters most.** AI-flavoured filler is a rejection vector and a human read is the cheap defence. Twelve guides, ~35 minutes of reading.
- [ ] Application submitted with ads dark; review-freeze observed; any rejection recorded + classified + acted on per the loop protocol. → **owner-gated.** `ADS_ENABLED` stays `false`; nothing has been submitted.
- [ ] **Terminal (external):** AdSense Sites page shows the domain **approved/ready**. Phase closes only on this; until then it parks at 🟠 with the loop live.

**Gate for this branch.** `ng test` **508 tests / 58 files** green (from 466 on `main`) · `ng lint` `All files pass linting` · `npm run build` **Prerendered 43 static routes** · `npm run verify:prerender` green · `./infra/test.sh --e2e` green including the new `phase-11.spec.ts` · `data-test` diff **0 removed, 15 added**. Lighthouse on the built bundle, desktop: **100 / 100 / 100** (accessibility, best practices, SEO) on the landing page, `/merge-pdf` and a guide.

### Phase 12 — Usability add-ons · ✅ Complete (2026-08-21)

Plan: [phase-12-usability-add-ons.md](phase-12-usability-add-ons.md). CLI handoff: **done** — [2026-08-21-phase-12-cli-handoff.md](../docs/archived/2026-08-21-phase-12-cli-handoff.md), executed end to end on the owner's Mac and archived with an Executed banner. Merged as **PR #20** (`ec8a33e`).

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-12-usability-add-ons.md, ticked only with evidence):

- [x] A right-click on the page no longer acts as a left-click (D-A): it does not select, deselect, drag, or delete anything. → `page-overlay.spec.ts` *"does not select, deselect or start a drag on a right-click"*, plus its sibling proving a left-click still does; `onPointerDown` / `onItemPointerDown` / `onHandlePointerDown` / `onTextPointerDown` all return on `event.button !== 0`.
- [x] Right-click on a mark, text block, image, link, form field, redaction area, signature placement or request field opens a menu whose every action works; where a surface offers nothing, the browser's own menu is left alone. → six component specs assert the action list per surface (`annotate`, `edit`, `forms`, `protect`, `sign`, `request-builder`); `page-overlay.spec.ts` *"leaves the browser's own menu alone when there is nothing to offer"* checks `defaultPrevented === false`. Driven live: Annotate's menu reads Copy · Cut · Duplicate · Edit comment… · Paste here · Delete; Protect's reads Remove area.
- [x] The menu is fully keyboard-operable — openable with `Shift+F10`, navigable with ↑/↓/Home/End, activated with Enter, dismissed with Esc — returns focus where it came from, and never lets a key through to the overlay beneath it. → `page-overlay.spec.ts` *"opens from the keyboard, anchored on the selection"*, *"walks past the disabled entry rather than landing on it"*, *"jumps with Home and End"*, *"activates on Enter and reports the item the menu belonged to"*, *"closes on Escape without also clearing the selection"*, *"closes on Tab"*, *"owns the keyboard entirely while it is open"*.
- [x] Every menu action is also reachable without a right-click, from a rail or a button. → Annotate's comment rows gained `comment-copy` / `comment-duplicate` and the palette an `annot-paste`; Protect, Sign and the request builder gained `area-list` / `placement-list` / `builder-field-list`, each row 44 px with a ✕.
- [x] ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z undo and redo in **all six** editing surfaces, and every locally-staged mutation is a single undo step. → `history.spec.ts` (8 cases) plus one per facade: `annotate.spec.ts` *"is one undo step per action"*, `edit.spec.ts` *"undoes and redoes one staged change at a time"*, `forms.spec.ts` *"undoes a staged field change in one step"*, `protect.spec.ts` *"brings a removed area back on undo"*, `sign.spec.ts` *"brings a removed placement back on undo"*, `request-builder.spec.ts` *"brings a removed field back on undo"*. Edit's button is titled "not yet saved" so its narrower scope is legible. **Corrected on the local gate (2026-08-21):** only Annotate, Edit and Forms actually *bound* the keys — Protect, Sign and the request builder had the buttons and the `aria-keyshortcuts` attribute announcing ⌘Z, and nothing listening for it. The three handlers are added here, each taking undo and redo only (`cancel`, `delete` and the nudges stay the overlay's, and neither Protect nor Sign takes ⌘S because "apply" destroys content rather than saving a session), with `protect.spec.ts` / `sign.spec.ts` / `request-builder.spec.ts` *"undoes and redoes from the keyboard, as the buttons say it does"* and a companion case proving the binding stays out of a focused field.
- [x] Copy, cut, paste and duplicate work on the selected item in Annotate and the Forms builder; a paste never collides with an existing field name; ⌘C with text selected still copies the text. → `annotate.spec.ts` (11 clipboard cases, including quads / ink / vertices moving as one piece and the author being dropped), `forms.spec.ts` *"gives a duplicate a name of its own"*, `shortcuts.spec.ts` *"leaves ⌘C and ⌘X alone when there is text selected to copy"*.
- [x] `Delete`/`Backspace` removes the selected item in every editing surface; arrow keys nudge it, cannot push it off the page, and still scroll the pane when nothing is selected. → `overlay-model.spec.ts` `nudgeRect` (4 cases), `page-overlay.spec.ts` nudging (7 cases incl. *"leaves the arrows to the scrolling pane when nothing is selected"*). Measured live: 10 presses moved a shape **15 px on a 600 px page** — exactly 0.25 % each — and Shift moved it ten times as far.
- [x] **Undo and Redo are visible controls** in Annotate, Edit, Forms, Protect, Sign and the request builder; the workspace bar carries both, two Undos in a row go back two versions (D-D), and Undo and Redo are never the same operation. → `data-test` `annot-undo/redo`, `edit-undo/redo`, `forms-undo/redo`, `protect-undo/redo`, `sign-undo/redo`, `builder-undo/redo`, `undo-version`, `redo-version`. `workspace-undo.spec.ts` (6 cases) proves the cursor: *"goes back one version at a time, and keeps going back"*, *"never lets Undo and Redo mean the same thing"*, *"cannot redo past where the chain started"*, *"ends the chain when any other operation appends a version"*.
- [x] A redaction area, a signature placement and a request field can each be selected, moved and removed — and a click no longer destroys any of them (D-B, D-C). The click-to-remove copy is gone from both `protect.html` and `sign.html`. → `protect.spec.ts` *"selects an area on a click instead of destroying it"*, `sign.spec.ts` *"can select a placement — which was not previously possible at all"*, `request-builder.spec.ts` *"selects a field on a click instead of deleting it"*; `moveArea` / `movePlacement` / the builder's field move each have a case. Driven live: after a click the area is **still there and outlined**, the menu removes it, and `protect-undo` brings it back.
- [x] `Mod+/` opens a shortcuts sheet listing exactly the implemented shortcuts, and no shortcut in the app is a bare printable character (SC 2.1.4). → `shortcuts.spec.ts` *"binds no printable character without a modifier"* (sweeps 49 characters, with and without Shift) and the bidirectional table test; `shortcuts-help.spec.ts` (5 cases). Driven live: **15 rows**, opened by `Ctrl+/` and by the bar button, closed by Esc.
- [x] Every new behaviour has unit tests; the suite is green and larger than 258; `ng lint` clean; `ng build` + 29 prerendered routes + `verify:prerender` green; zero `data-test` attributes removed. → **396 tests across 47 files** (from 258 across 38; 391 in the cloud, plus the five the local gate added for the three keyboard bindings) · `All files pass linting` · `Prerendered 29 static routes` · `verify-prerender: 24 tool pages server-rendered with unique title/H1, FAQ + SoftwareApplication JSON-LD, canonical, sitemap and robots` · `data-test` diff **0 removed, 30 added**. *(Corrected 2026-08-22, from the independent production audit: this said **27**, which is what the handoff's own command returns because it globs `frontend/src/**/*.html`. `ShortcutsHelp` carries its template inline in a `.ts` file, so `shortcuts-help`, `shortcut-row` and `shortcuts-close` were never in scope. The criterion itself — zero removed — held at either scope; only the "added" figure was short.)*
- [x] The contract amendments are in `design-instructions.md` in the same change. → §3 gains a **Context menu** component spec; §3 headers gain Redo + Shortcuts on the workspace bar; §3 modals gain the sheet; §3 workspace panes gain the right-click-reachability rule; §4 gains per-mode Undo/Redo and nudging; §6 gains the no-single-character rule, the `aria-keyshortcuts` format, the overlay host's roles and the 44 px menu floor; §8 gains the physical-placement exception with its reason.
- [x] Verified in a real browser in **both themes** at desktop and phone widths. → the built bundle served locally with production's MIME map and `nosniff`, `/api` proxied to the live Railway API. Nine screenshots (light + dark, 1440 + 390 px), **zero console errors**, no sideways scroll at 390 px, menu items measured at **44 px** and the menu confirmed inside the page box. **Re-done on the local stack** for the handoff's §2, against `localhost:4200` as an anonymous guest: ⌘C ⌘V offset the copy by 12 × 17 px on a 600 px page; the menu came up with six entries, every row `getBoundingClientRect().height === 44`; `↓`/`End` moved the highlight *and* `document.activeElement` and nudged nothing beneath; Esc closed it, kept the selection and returned focus to `APP-PAGE-OVERLAY`; one ArrowRight moved a shape **1.5 px**, Shift **14 px**, and at the edge `selection.right === overlay.right === 988.5` with the live region reading *"At the edge of the page"*; ⌘/ gave **15 rows** and Esc put focus back on `shortcuts-open`; Protect's area survived a click and showed its outline; a Sign placement was made, listed, ✕-removed and restored by `sign-undo`; the bar went v7 → shown v6 → shown v5 on two Undos. At 390 px `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 390` in both themes, **zero console errors** (the only warnings are pdf.js's pre-existing fluent strings for the sidebar buttons we hide).
- [x] **The local gate: `./infra/test.sh --e2e`, `ruff` + `mypy` + `pytest`, and the production smoke.** → run on the owner's Mac, 2026-08-21. Infra deny-list drift check: `deny-list identical across 4 infra copies`. `ruff check . && mypy apps config`: **All checks passed!**. `pytest -q` (`config.settings.test`): **1061 passed, 4 skipped in 107.11s** — no Python in the diff, so this is the no-new-failures check. `ng lint`: `All files pass linting`. `ng test --watch=false`: **47 files, 396 tests, 0 failed**. `npm run build`: `Prerendered 29 static routes`. `npm run verify:prerender`: green. **Playwright, whole suite: 63 passed, 0 failed** — including `phase-2b:130`, which the handoff listed as a known pre-existing failure and which passes now that the Celery workers are running current code. *(Qualified 2026-08-22: this said `infra/up.sh` "has restarted the Celery workers", which reads as a guarantee and is not one. `up.sh` runs `docker compose up -d --build` (`infra/up.sh:30`), and compose recreates a container **only when its image or definition changed** — on a volume-mounted dev image a pure backend code change recreates nothing. It happened to recreate them this time. The explicit restart that would make it a guarantee is added to `test.sh` by `docs/reviews/handoffs/handoff-to-cli-e2e-gate-hardening.md`.)* Production smoke below.

**Production smoke (2026-08-21, after the merge).** All eight Railway services and both databases **● Online**; `/api/health/` `{"status":"ok"}` with db, redis, storage, gotenberg and workers all true. Production serves **`main-ZV5ZVVU6.js`** — the same hash `npm run build` produced here, so the bundle under test is the bundle that shipped. Driven live at 1440 px as an anonymous guest: `/annotate-pdf` → upload → workspace → draw a rectangle → **right-click it → a five-entry menu** (Copy · Cut · Duplicate · Edit comment… · Delete — no *Paste here*, correctly, because the clipboard is empty and an empty action list means no entry) with every row measuring **44 px**; `⌘/` opened the **15-row** sheet; the bar carries Undo, Redo and Shortcuts. **The console is empty — not one message of any level.**

**Plan review.** The plan was written, then read back against the built code by two independent adversarial passes — one on factual accuracy, one on the design contract and accessibility. **47 findings, 14 HIGH**; all material ones folded in before a line of code. The four that changed the shape of the phase: the overlay never tested `event.button`, so a context menu could not be built on it safely (D-A); pdf.js binds `Ctrl/⌘+S`, `+`, `-`, `PageUp`/`PageDown` on `window` whenever the viewer is mounted, which retired four proposed bindings; every single-character shortcut fails WCAG SC 2.1.4, which retired `?` in favour of `⌘/`; and a right-click menu with no rail equivalent is unreachable on a phone, which is what added the Areas / Placements / Fields lists. The review also caught two `PageOverlay` consumers the draft had missed — the sign request builder (same destructive click, now fixed) and Compare (read-only, explicitly out of scope).


### Phase 10 — Hardening & Release · 🟠 Awaiting human review (2026-08-02)

**This phase ends amber by design.** The engineering is done and evidenced
below; what remains needs a domain, an account, a card or a signature, and is
listed in `docs/10-launch-checklist.md`. **`v1.0.0` is not tagged**, because the
checklist has unticked owner items and a version number is a claim about all of
them.

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-10-hardening-release.md):
- [~] Hostile-corpus suite: zero worker crashes that don't recycle cleanly; zero cross-user leaks; all documented limits enforced. → **The corpus and the isolation sweep are done; half the recycle clause now is too.** *(Updated 2026-08-02 after the debt pass: the soft-limit path is tested — a killed job fails with a readable message rather than escaping as `SoftTimeLimitExceeded(60,)`, both kill paths say the same sentence, and a redelivered `running` job is failed rather than handed back to another worker, which was "queue drains" failing under exactly the named fixture. The hard-kill-and-recycle half still needs a real worker and is a runbook drill.)* `apps/pdf_engine/tests/test_hostile_corpus.py` ships five fixtures built by the committed generator — a self-referential page tree, a 10 000-deep outline, an 80 MB decompression bomb, a `.docx` declaring a gigabyte, a malformed TIFF — each asserting a **time and memory budget**, because a guard that answers after ten minutes is not a guard. The zip bomb found a real gap and closed it: `convert.check_archive` reads the central directory *before* LibreOffice is handed the file. Isolation: `apps/core/tests/test_isolation_sweep.py` walks the URL table rather than the routes somebody remembered — every id-bearing `AllowAny` route must be listed with a written reason; sign requests, audit, saved signatures, folders, job downloads and thumbnails all answer 404 (never 403) to another account; a completed recipient cannot sign twice and a canceled request's token is 410. **What is not proven:** that a worker *killed mid-attack* recycles cleanly and leaves the job `failed`. The fixtures run the engine in-process, so they demonstrate the guard, not the kill path — that is `reap_stalled_jobs`' territory and it has its own tests. Queued.
- [~] Lighthouse (landing + dashboard) ≥90 perf/a11y/best-practices/SEO on prod build. → **Landing, measured: accessibility 100, best practices 100, SEO 100** — but against the dev server, and this tooling does not score performance without a trace. *(Corrected 2026-08-22: the original text said "there is no deployed host yet", which stopped being true on 2026-08-08. **A production host has existed since then** — `https://zenpdf.up.railway.app`, `docs/ops/railway-deploy-report-2026-08-08.md`, auto-deploying from `main` since 08-10. So this criterion is no longer **blocked**; it is **owed**. The landing run can be pointed at the deployed bundle today; the dashboard half still needs an authenticated browser session, which is the only remaining obstacle. Owner: `docs/reviews/handoffs/handoff-to-cli-launch-gate-evidence.md`.)* `ng build --configuration=production` succeeds and prerenders 29 routes.
- [x] axe-core zero serious/critical across routes; keyboard-only ceremony pass recorded. → *(Was `[~]`; completed 2026-08-02 in the debt pass.)* `e2e/tests/phase-10-a11y.spec.ts` scans seven public routes, the dashboard empty and populated, the workspace viewer and its annotate/edit/protect/convert panels, settings, the delete confirmation and four of the ceremony's seven screens plus the signature dialog — zero serious or critical. It found real barriers, all fixed: `text-slate-400` body text at 2.86:1 across most of the product, white on `emerald-600` buttons and toasts at 3.65:1, unlabelled inputs on both auth screens and in settings, an unlabelled per-document checkbox, links distinguishable by colour alone, body text at 4.1:1 on the convert panel, and a disclosure box a mouse could scroll and a keyboard could not — on the screen where somebody agrees to sign. Every dialog traps focus (CDK), names itself and answers Escape; toasts are a live region; `prefers-reduced-motion` is honoured. **The keyboard test now completes an envelope** — types a signature, picks a font, applies it, presses Finish — with Tab, Enter and Space only; the deferral reason turned out to be untrue, the typed path already worked and was merely untested. `docs/10-accessibility-screen-reader-script.md` is the manual half: sixteen steps ending with "turn off your monitor and repeat steps 8–15", owner-executed and on the launch checklist. **Not scanned, and worth saying:** `/legal/terms`, the e-sign disclosure, the unsubscribe and verify-email pages, the 404, the two sign-request builder routes, and the ceremony's `wait`/`closed` screens. The keyboard test asserts the done screen, which the component also shows while finalizing — so it proves the signer got there, not that the envelope reached `completed`. And what axe structurally could not see, `angular-eslint` did — twelve unlabelled controls behind panel tabs the scan never opens. PDF.js's own toolbar stays excluded, with the reason in the spec.
- [~] `@full` suite green 3 consecutive nightly runs on the prod-shaped stack. → **Green on the dev-shaped stack; not yet three nightly runs.** The suites and the cadence exist (`docs/ops/release.md`, `@smoke` ~30 s as the deploy gate, `BROWSERS=all` for the nightly cross-browser run), and the full suite is green on a fresh stack (63/63 on 2026-08-21). "Three consecutive nightly runs" is a claim only time can make. *(Corrected 2026-08-22: the original text also said "the prod-shaped stack needs the host from the checklist" — the host exists, and `@smoke` has been run against it (2026-08-21: 4 passed, 2 environmental). Nothing blocks the cadence but the cadence itself. Owner: `handoff-to-cli-launch-gate-evidence.md`.)*
- [~] Clean-VM prod compose deploy documented + performed; restore drill performed. → **Re-scoped 2026-08-22, and half done.** A **production deploy was performed on Railway 2026-08-08** (`docs/ops/railway-deploy-report-2026-08-08.md`), auto-deploying from `main` since 08-10 and serving the current `main` today. **A compose deploy on a clean VM was never performed and is no longer the target** — the criterion was written before the deploy target was chosen, and re-running it now would prove something about a stack nobody operates. **The restore drill is still owed** and is the real remaining half (`docs/ops/restore-drill.md`, whose Railway path is a volume snapshot plus a managed-Postgres restore, keeping the audit-chain re-verification step — a restore that breaks the chain has destroyed the evidence the product exists to produce). Documentation stands: `docs/ops/deploy.md`, `rollback.md` and `restore-drill.md` are written to be followed at 3am by somebody who did not build this, and `manage.py check --deploy` is clean under `config.settings.prod` (the two remaining warnings are a throwaway `SECRET_KEY` in the check command and HSTS preload, which is deliberately owner-opt-in). The drill needs a Railway token — owner item, `docs/reviews/status-review-2026-08-21.md` §5.2.
- [~] p95 budgets met; queue-starvation test passes. → **Shape pinned; the number is owed, and since 2026-08-08 it is runnable.** *(Corrected 2026-08-22: the trailing sentence below says the `locust` run "belongs with the deployed host" as though there were none. There is — Railway since 08-08. The run needs `PERF_EMAIL` pointed at a real account on production and a decision about running a load generator against a live service; it is **owed, not blocked**. Owner: `handoff-to-cli-launch-gate-evidence.md`.)* *(Updated 2026-08-02: `infra/perf/` is a committed, profile-gated locust smoke that runs in-network, turns the throttles off for the run rather than raising them, and fails on any 429 — a p95 over rate-limit rejections is not a p95. It reports 3–76 ms against the 150 ms budget on a laptop with a four-document library, which proves the shape, not the figure. All six §10.2 queries are audited on the real planner via `./infra/test.sh --pg`; two needed an index and two needed nothing.)* Originally: `apps/core/tests/test_performance.py` seeds 3 000 documents and asserts the library list uses the covering index rather than sorting everything (the EXPLAIN audit found `(owner, trashed_at)` did not cover `ORDER BY updated_at`; both principals now have `(owner, trashed_at, -updated_at)`, and the audit chain has `(sign_request, created_at)`), that listing stays paginated, and that the lanes stay separated so an hour of OCR cannot stop somebody rotating a page. A p95 figure from a laptop is not a p95 figure from production, so the `locust` run belongs with the deployed host.
- [ ] Launch checklist 100% with owner sign-offs (legal content, ads readiness, domain). → **`docs/10-launch-checklist.md`, unticked.** This is the GATE.

**What shipped**

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Structured logs + correlation | `apps/core/logging.py` — request id bound in middleware, **sanitised** (it lands in log lines), echoed on every response, carried into Celery through message headers; JSON in production, readable in dev. `test_observability.py` (12 tests) |
| Health split | `/api/health/live` is dependency-free — a liveness probe that checks Postgres restarts the API whenever Postgres hiccups — and `/api/health/` reports queue depths and a worker heartbeat, because a green stack with dead workers looks identical from outside |
| Error reporting | Sentry wired everywhere, active nowhere without a DSN, PII scrubbed at the SDK boundary: no credentials, no request body, no cookies, no address. This product handles other people's contracts |
| Security headers | `frontend/nginx.conf` — CSP (ads-off policy, with the ad hosts written down for the day they are needed), HSTS, nosniff, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, Permissions-Policy. Repeated per location, because nginx's `add_header` replaces rather than inherits — silently dropping them on `/api/` is where user content is served. COOP/COEP deliberately not set, with the reason recorded |
| Admin, gated | Mounted at a configurable path behind a source-IP allowlist that **denies when unconfigured** and answers 404 rather than 403. Phase 9 built moderation actions production could not reach |
| Privacy | Account deletion and data export exist rather than being promised. Deletion asks for the password; a completed envelope **survives** the account that sent it, with the sender snapshotted as text at send time so the certificate still prints, and the audit chain deliberately not rewritten |
| Dependencies | `pip-audit` flagged 18 advisories in Pillow's image decoders — on a product whose job is decoding files somebody else uploaded. Pillow 12.3.0, requests 2.33.0, pytest 9.0.3; both audits now clean |
| Runbooks | `docs/ops/` — deploy, rollback, restore drill, queue stuck, storage full, cert renewal, release |

**Decisions** (also in the Decisions log):
- **A completed signature envelope outlives the account that sent it.** `SignRequest.owner` and `.document` became `SET_NULL`, and the sender is snapshotted as text at send. The counterparty signed a contract; deleting their evidence because the other party closed an account is the wrong answer, and the privacy policy now says exactly what is kept.
- **The audit chain is never rewritten, including by deletion.** Editing one row breaks every hash after it and destroys the thing being kept.
- **Zero retries in Playwright.** A test that passes on the second attempt is telling you something about the product, and a retry count is how that stops being heard.
- **Cross-browser is opt-in.** Three engines on every run triples a suite that drives real PDFs through a real worker; the failures we have actually hit are logic, not engine. The nightly run sets `BROWSERS=all`.

It also said each `[~]` "needs the deployed host"; the host has existed since 2026-08-08, so what the four host-shaped ones need now is a run, a session or a token — see the criteria themselves.)* |

### Phase 9 — Ads & Abuse Controls · ✅ Complete (2026-08-02)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-09-ads-and-abuse-controls.md):
- [x] With `ADS_ENABLED=false` (default): zero ad code loaded, product fully functional — launchable state. → `/api/config/` answers `ads: {enabled: false}` and **nothing else** — no client id, no provider, no slot ids — so a build with the flag off has nothing to load even by mistake (`test_ads_and_abuse.py::test_ads_off_ships_no_client_id_no_slots`). e2e `phase-9.spec.ts::with ads off, no ad code loads and nothing asks for consent` enumerates every `script[src]` on the landing page and asserts none is `adsbygoogle`/`googlesyndication`, that no banner appears, and that a tool page still works. `/ads.txt` is rendered from `ADSENSE_CLIENT_ID` (nginx proxies the site root to the API), and with no id it says so rather than naming a seller: `::test_ads_txt_is_rendered_from_the_publisher_id`.
- [x] With ads on + consent granted: slots render in the three allowed surfaces only; ceremony/editor/verify provably ad-free. → `ad-slot.spec.ts` (9 tests): nothing renders with ads off, nothing renders before a decision, nothing renders after a decline, nothing renders on a forbidden route, the box is reserved at its declared height *before* the ad arrives, and the provider script is appended exactly once across several slots. The exclusion is code, not convention — `AdSlot.FORBIDDEN = ['/s/', '/verify', '/legal/', '/app/doc']` is asserted, **including the editor/viewer canvas**, and e2e `::with ads on and consent granted, slots appear — and only there` grants consent with ads stubbed on, sees the landing slot, then visits `/verify` and `/legal/privacy` and asserts zero ad markup. Placements: dashboard rail (≥1280px), one inline card in the list below that, tool-completion panel, landing.
- [~] Consent banner appears for EEA-simulated visitors; declining yields non-personalized ads; choice persisted. → **Declining yields *no* ads, not non-personalized ones** — the deliberate deviation recorded in the Decisions log below, because serving NPA correctly under TCF needs the certified CMP's signal and guessing at it is a compliance failure rather than a lost impression. Everything else is ticked: the region rule is one server-side list (`CONSENT_REQUIRED_REGIONS`) tested across nine regions including "no region at all → ask" (`::test_consent_is_required_where_it_is_required`); the client reports its **IANA timezone** rather than its locale (`consent.service.spec.ts` — `en-US` in `Europe/Berlin` must still be `DE`, or most EU visitors would never see the banner); Consent Mode's default is `denied` for every ad signal before any tag can load; and a decline is stored, survives a reload, and **unloads the tag** (`ConsentService.teardown()`), because a consent that cannot be withdrawn is not consent. e2e `::declining is a real answer and loads nothing`.
- [x] Legal pages live, linked, and matching real system behavior (retention numbers cross-checked against beat config in a test). → `/legal/privacy`, `/legal/terms`, `/about` (plus `/legal/esign-disclosure` from Phase 8), prerendered, in `sitemap.xml`, and linked from a shared footer on the landing page, every tool page, the dashboard and the ceremony. The cross-check has two halves because the pages **prerender**, where no API call is possible: `::test_the_retention_numbers_come_from_the_settings_the_sweepers_use` asserts the API payload equals `TRASH_RETENTION_DAYS` / `GUEST_TTL_HOURS` / `EXPORT_TTL_HOURS` and that each has a beat task enforcing it, and e2e `::the legal pages are live, linked, and quote the real numbers` reads `frontend/src/app/core/retention.ts` — the constant the prerendered HTML is built from — off disk and asserts it equals the live `/api/config/` payload *and* what the page displays. `::test_trash_purge_removes_what_the_policy_says_it_removes` proves the 30-day sweep exists rather than being a sentence.
- [x] Unverified accounts cannot send sign requests (uploading stays open — guests can upload, so accounts must too); verified flow smooth (<1 min via Mailpit locally). → `test_sign_api.py::test_an_unverified_account_cannot_send_but_can_build` (403 `email_not_verified`, and **no mail sent**) beside `test_ads_and_abuse.py::test_an_unverified_account_can_still_do_everything_else` (upload 201, operation 202). Registration now mails the link — without that the gate was unreachable from a cold start — and the refusal is answered *where it happens*: e2e `::an unverified account is told why it cannot send yet` asserts the inline panel, that the built request is still on screen, and that the resend button gives an honest answer (sent, or the cooldown). `::an account verifies its address through the inbox` does the whole loop through Mailpit in about two seconds.
- [x] Ads render for a guest with consent granted, and the CMP consent flow completes with no account. → The consent path touches no account at all: the banner, the choice and the slots are all driven by `/api/config/` + `localStorage`, and the e2e specs that exercise them run in a cleared context with no registration anywhere in the path. `ad-slot.spec.ts` drives the same code with a guest-principal config payload.
- [x] All throttle/quota limits return the standard error shape with human-readable messaging in the UI. → Every limit raises a `ZenAPIException`, so the §6 shape is structural: `{"error": {code, message, details}}` plus `Retry-After`. Phase 9's matrix, each exercised: `upload` 20/hour per **account** and **only on writes** (`::test_uploading_too_fast_is_refused_with_a_retry_after` asserts the 429 *and* that the library is still readable — the first cut throttled GETs and locked a user out of their own files in twenty page loads); `verify` 10/min per IP behind a 60/hour ceiling (`::test_verification_is_capped_per_hour_as_well_as_per_minute`); `public_sign` 20/min per IP **plus 200/day per token** (`::test_public_signing_is_capped_per_token_as_well_as_per_ip`); `auth` 10/min per IP; and a 120 s per-account cooldown on the verification mail (`::test_the_verification_mail_has_a_cooldown`) — that mail is transactional, so the suppression list cannot stop it and the cooldown is the cap.

**Decisions** (also in the Decisions log):
- **`List-Unsubscribe` points at the API, and the token carries a keyed hash rather than the address.** RFC 8058 one-click is a server-to-server POST with no JavaScript in sight; aimed at an Angular route it returned the app shell, suppressed nothing, and told the person they had unsubscribed. A browser GET now redirects to a page that *asks* — the link is printed in every footer and travels with a forwarded message, so one that fires on load is a kill switch anybody downstream can pull — and the suppression is reversible from that page and from admin.
- **A suppressed invitation is not a notified recipient.** `mail.send` returns a count and every caller honours it: the recipient is left un-notified and the audit event records who was actually reached. An e-signature audit trail that asserts a delivery the suppression list swallowed is the wrong kind of wrong.
- **Declining consent means no ads, not non-personalized ads** (see the criterion above), and outside the consent regions — where nothing is asked — ads *do* load. Gating on an explicit `granted` everywhere would have meant an ad-funded product that never shows one.
- **The abuse report is answered without the reporter's request context.** `record()` stores `client_ip`, and the audit trail is readable by the owner — so the first cut handed the person being reported their reporter's IP address. The report row keeps it for staff. The response also no longer returns the running count: telling each reporter how close they are to pausing somebody's contract is an invitation to organise.
- **Turnstile sits on register and on *completing* a signature**, not on opening a signing link: somebody who was legitimately sent a document should not meet a puzzle before they can read it. Behind `CAPTCHA_ENABLED`, off in dev and test.

**Definition of Done (§20), on a fresh stack** — `./infra/reset.sh --yes && ./infra/up.sh && ./infra/test.sh --e2e`:

| DoD item | Result |
|---|---|
| 1. Acceptance criteria on a fresh stack | ✅ 6 ticked, 1 `[~]` (declined consent means no ads rather than NPA until the certified CMP is wired — recorded, and the readiness doc says so) |
| 2. Migrations idempotent from zero | ✅ fresh volumes → all applied; Phase 9 adds two (`esign.0002`: `AbuseReport` + `canceled_by_abuse`; `core.0004`: the suppression list keyed on a hash) |
| 3. Tests green; coverage gates | ✅ backend **905 passed** (was 870), frontend **178 passed** (was 163), e2e **56 passed** (was 43). Coverage: `apps` **91%** (gate 85), `pdf_engine` **92%** (gate 90) |
| 4. Playwright happy path | ✅ `e2e/tests/phase-9.spec.ts` — 10 specs, including the usage panel asserted *after* a real OCR run |
| 5. OpenAPI updated & accurate | ✅ `spectacular --fail-on-warn` → 0/0; verification, unsubscribe (GET/POST/DELETE), `/api/ads.txt` and report all documented |
| 6. Lint clean | ✅ `ruff` + `manage.py check` clean. `mypy`/`eslint` remain the repo-wide debt Phase 10 owns |
| 7. No TODOs / dead code | ✅ none added; `frontend/public/ads.txt` removed, because the file is now rendered from config |
| 8. PROGRESS.md updated | ✅ this section |
| 9. Guest-usable tools + public tool pages | ✅ unchanged and re-asserted: the consent flow, the legal pages and `/verify` all work with no account, and the verification gate touches **only** sending for signature and claiming above the free storage tier |

### Phase 8 — E-Signatures · ✅ Complete (2026-08-02)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-08-esignatures.md):
- [x] Self-sign in <4 clicks from an open document. → **Three.** The panel opens with the signature dialog already up, because there is nothing to pick from on a first visit: draw (1) → drag the box on the page (2) → Apply (3). e2e `phase-8.spec.ts::a guest signs a PDF from /sign-pdf with no login anywhere` performs exactly those three and asserts the version label `Signed (self)`; the count is in the spec's own comments so it fails if the flow grows a step.
- [~] Full 2-signer sequential flow works on a phone-sized viewport without login. → **Driven at 390×844 in Playwright's Chromium, not on a physical phone.** e2e `::two signers in order, sealed, certified and verifiable` opens each ceremony in its own context with `viewport: { width: 390, height: 844 }` and completes the whole loop — consent, draw, finish, then type, finish — with no login anywhere in either context. Real-device touch (a finger on the canvas, iOS Safari's viewport quirks) is an owner check; queued.
- [x] Completed PDF opens in Acrobat/any validator showing an intact signature; ANY byte modification flips verification to invalid (test proves). → `test_sign_api.py::test_the_full_two_signer_loop_completes_and_seals` seals with pyHanko, re-reads with `validate_pdf_signature`, and asserts `integrity: intact`, `whole_document: True`; then flips **one byte** inside a page object and asserts `integrity: modified`. `::test_verify_flags_a_modified_file` does the same through the public `/api/verify/` endpoint, which also reports `sha256_match: false`. "Opens in Acrobat" is the same `[~]` class as Phase 3's — the validator we run is pyHanko, which is the reference implementation, and a human eye on Acrobat is queued.
- [x] Audit trail + certificate reproduce every event with IP/UA/UTC and chain integrity. → `AuditEvent` is a hash chain: `test_routing.py::test_the_chain_verifies_as_written`, `::test_tampering_with_an_event_breaks_the_chain` (a queryset `update()` of one IP breaks it and names the row), `::test_deleting_an_event_breaks_the_chain`, `::test_an_audit_event_cannot_be_edited_or_deleted_through_the_model`, `::test_two_requests_keep_separate_chains`. The certificate: `test_sign_api.py::test_the_certificate_reproduces_every_event` extracts the generated PDF's text and asserts the envelope code, the post-seal SHA-256, both signer addresses, every event type (Sent/Opened/Consented/Signed/Completed) and the line "The chain verifies." IP and user-agent are recorded per event (`::test_consent_records_the_exact_disclosure_it_agreed_to` asserts both are present) and printed on the certificate.
- [x] Consent is unskippable and its exact disclosure text is versioned in the repo (legal page + hash recorded in audit metadata). → The text is `apps/esign/legal.py::DISCLOSURE` with a dated `VERSION`; its SHA-256 goes into the chain with every consent event (`::test_consent_records_the_exact_disclosure_it_agreed_to` compares the stored hash against `disclosure_hash()`). Unskippable at the API: `::test_nothing_can_be_filled_before_consent` gets 400 on a field POST and 200 only after consenting. Unskippable in the UI: the ceremony's continue button is `disabled` until the box is ticked, asserted in e2e.
- [x] Reminders/expiry fire per beat schedule (time-warped tests). → `::test_reminders_respect_the_cadence` ages `last_notified_at` by four days and asserts `sign_reminders()` goes from 0 to 1, with the email and the `reminder_sent` event; `::test_expiry_closes_the_request_and_tells_everyone` back-dates `expires_at` and asserts the status flips to `expired` and that both the owner and the pending signer are told. `::test_a_manual_nudge_is_limited_to_one_a_day` pins the owner-triggered button's own limit. Both tasks are on the beat schedule at hourly cadence.
- [x] Monthly sign-request quota enforced (§16). → `::test_the_monthly_quota_is_enforced` overrides the free tier to 1, sends one, and asserts the second create is 429 `quota_exceeded`. Resolved through `settings.TIERS` / `core.limits.enforce_sign_requests()` like every other limit; a tier whose row is 0 (guest) raises `account_required` instead, because "you are out of allowance" would be a lie about a feature they never had.

**Phase-2B GATE — cleared.** Both carried criteria are now demonstrated:
- Guest `self_sign` via `signature_upload_ref`: `test_self_sign.py::test_a_guest_signs_a_document_with_no_account` (upload → op → `Signed (self)` version → the image is on the page and the date is stamped), and e2e `::a guest signs a PDF from /sign-pdf with no login anywhere`.
- Guest sign-request → 403 `account_required`: `test_sign_api.py::test_a_guest_cannot_send_a_signature_request` asserts the code, the signup-prompt copy, and that **no row is created**. Phase 2B's own tripwire (`test_guest_abuse.py::test_no_guest_reachable_route_accepts_a_recipient_address`) was written to fail once these routes existed; it now resolves every `sign-request` route to its view and asserts `IsAccount` is declared.

### Phase 7 — Security & Redaction · ✅ Complete (2026-08-02)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-07-security-redaction.md):
- [x] **Guest parity + tool pages (§20 DoD item 9, §21.6):** every tool in this phase works end-to-end with no account, and ships its public SSR page — `/protect-pdf` `/unlock-pdf` `/redact-pdf` — with unique title/meta/H1 and an entry in the generated `sitemap.xml`. → Guest parity per op: `test_security_api.py::test_a_guest_protects_and_unlocks` (encrypt **and** decrypt), `::test_a_guest_redacts`, `::test_a_guest_sanitizes`. e2e `phase-7.spec.ts::a guest protects a PDF from /protect-pdf with no login prompt` and `::a guest redacts from /redact-pdf, landing on the redact tab` run cold contexts through tool page → workspace → operation, asserting `login-form` absent throughout; `::the three new tool pages are reachable and self-describing` visits all three. `node tools/generate-seo.mjs` → *"wrote sitemap.xml (23 tool pages)"*; `tool-pages.spec.ts` + `seo-artifacts.spec.ts` enforce table↔routes↔prerender↔sitemap parity (`ng build` → *"Prerendered 24 static routes"*).
- [~] Password-protect + unlock round-trip through UI; permissions visibly enforced in external viewers (spot-check print-restricted output). → **Round-trip: ticked. External viewer: an owner check** (added to the Human review queue), for the same reason Phase 3's Acrobat item is — "the print button is greyed out in Preview" is a rendering judgement no test makes. Round-trip through the UI: e2e `phase-7.spec.ts::protect a document, reopen it, unlock it, then work on it` sets both passwords (with the strength meter and the mismatch warning), protects, reloads into the password prompt, unlocks, and removes the password. The *bits* are asserted rather than assumed: `test_security.py::test_print_permission_levels_land_on_the_right_bits` and `::test_modify_permission_levels_land_on_the_right_bits` read `pikepdf`'s `allow` back for all three print levels and all four modify levels, `::test_accessibility_is_never_restricted` proves the one flag we refuse to turn off, and `test_security_api.py::test_permissions_can_be_changed` does it through the API.
- [x] Pattern redaction on the PII fixture removes content irrecoverably (extraction + raw-bytes grep in test), clean-copy default prevents history leakage. → `test_redact.py::test_pattern_redaction_removes_the_content_irrecoverably` — and the grep is a *real* one: `raw_text_bytes()` inflates every content stream and decodes hex string literals first, because PyMuPDF writes text as hex inside deflated streams and the naive `b"secret" not in pdf_bytes` was true **before** redaction too. The test asserts the fixture contains the string to begin with, so it cannot pass vacuously. History leakage: `test_security_api.py::test_the_clean_copy_has_no_history_to_leak` (the fork has exactly one version and the original is untouched — which is what makes it a copy); the UI defaults the checkbox on and explains why, e2e `::redact every email but one, and prove the text is gone` applies it and then proves removal through the app's own find bar (`dana.cohen` → 0 results, the unticked `r.levi` → 1).
- [x] Redaction of a region overlapping an image blacks out image content, not just overlay. → `test_redact.py::test_redacting_over_an_image_destroys_the_pixels` renders the page before and after and asserts the red pixels of the fixture photograph are **gone from the raster**, not merely covered — `apply_redactions(images=fitz.PDF_REDACT_IMAGE_PIXELS)`. Fixture `redact-image.pdf`, built by the committed generator.
- [x] Sanitize report accurate against a booby-trapped fixture (JS + attachment + metadata). → `test_security.py::test_sanitize_removes_the_booby_traps_and_counts_them` checks the file with **pikepdf** afterwards (no `/OpenAction`, no `/Names/JavaScript`, no `/EmbeddedFiles`, empty docinfo, no `/Metadata`) *and* the counts in the report; `::test_sanitize_only_removes_what_was_asked_for` proves the checklist is a checklist; `::test_sanitize_removes_comments_when_asked` asserts the note's text is gone from the **bytes**, which is what forced the `/Popup` removal and the `garbage=4` compaction pass. API: `test_security_api.py::test_sanitize_reports_what_it_removed`; e2e `::sanitize reports what it actually removed` reads the counts out of the toast.
- [x] Session-password ops work without re-prompting per action. → The password lives in `DocumentPasswords` (memory only) and is attached by `DocumentsService.operation()` to **every** operation, not by each facade — `security.facade.spec.ts::sends the session password with every operation, so nothing re-prompts` and `::holds the session password in memory only` (asserts it is in neither `localStorage` nor `document.cookie`). Backend: `test_security_api.py::test_the_session_password_lets_ordinary_work_continue` (a `rotate_pages` on an encrypted document, 202 → succeeded). e2e `::editing an unlocked document says the password came off` rotates a page with no prompt in sight (`pdf-password` count 0).

**Decisions** (also in the Decisions log):
- **Editing an unlocked document takes its protection off, and says so.** The engine works on decrypted bytes; neither password is recoverable from the other, so the original encryption cannot be put back faithfully, and re-encrypting with the one password we were given would quietly make it both. So the flag flips, the version label reads *"… (password removed)"*, and the banner says it **before** the user acts. `test_security_api.py::test_editing_an_unlocked_document_says_the_password_came_off`.
- **Guessing is throttled per document, not per principal** (5/min): the thing being guessed at belongs to the document, and an attacker with several sessions would otherwise get five tries each. Charged in the worker, where the guess is known to be wrong; refused at the view. `::test_guessing_is_throttled_per_document`.
- **The PII fixture gained a third email address.** The review list only earns its existence if a user can keep some matches and drop others, and two is not enough to show that (the phase's own E2E says "review shows 3, uncheck 1, apply 2").
- **`THROTTLE_AUTH` is relaxed in `config.settings.dev` only.** It is per IP, and in dev the whole e2e suite is one IP; 10/min meant the eleventh test to register an account failed for a reason unrelated to what it tested. Production keeps the real rate.

|---|---|

| 2. Migrations idempotent from zero | ✅ fresh volumes → all migrations applied; Phase 7 adds **no** migration (passwords are never persisted — that is the point) |
| 3. Tests green; coverage gates | ✅ backend **800 passed** (was 681), frontend **153 passed** (was 133), e2e **38 passed** (was 29). Coverage: `apps` **91%** (gate 85), `pdf_engine` **92%** (gate 90) |
| 4. Playwright happy path | ✅ `e2e/tests/phase-7.spec.ts` — 9 specs |
| 5. OpenAPI updated & accurate | ✅ `manage.py spectacular --fail-on-warn` → **0 warnings, 0 errors**; `document_password` documented as a write-only sibling of `params` |
| 6. Lint clean | ✅ `ruff` clean; `manage.py check` clean. `mypy`/`eslint` remain the repo-wide debt Phase 10 owns |

### Phase 6 — OCR, Conversion & Compare · ✅ Complete (2026-08-02)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-06-ocr-conversion-compare.md):
- [x] **Guest parity + tool pages (§20 DoD item 9, §21.6):** every tool in this phase works end-to-end with no account, and ships its public SSR page — `/ocr-pdf` `/pdf-to-word` `/word-to-pdf` `/jpg-to-pdf` `/pdf-to-jpg` `/html-to-pdf` `/compare-pdf` `/repair-pdf` — with unique title/meta/H1 and an entry in the generated `sitemap.xml`. → Guest parity per op: `test_convert_api.py::test_a_guest_runs_ocr`, `::test_a_guest_exports_and_downloads`, `::test_a_guest_imports_an_image`, `::test_a_guest_compares_two_of_its_own_documents`, `::test_a_guest_repairs_a_document`. e2e `phase-6.spec.ts` drives three of the eight pages as a cold guest end-to-end (`::a guest OCRs a scan from /ocr-pdf`, `::converts a PDF to Word from /pdf-to-word`, `::turns an image into a PDF from /jpg-to-pdf`) and `::every new tool page is reachable and self-describing` visits all eight, asserting the H1, the dropzone and the absence of any login form. `node tools/generate-seo.mjs` → *"wrote sitemap.xml (20 tool pages)"*; `tool-pages.spec.ts` + `seo-artifacts.spec.ts` enforce table↔routes↔prerender↔sitemap parity.
- [x] Scanned fixture: OCR completes <60 s/10 pages, text selectable, P4 editor now allowed on it. → `test_ocr.py::test_ten_pages_finish_inside_the_budget` **measures** the elapsed time on a 10-page scan and asserts `< 60`. Text selectable: `::test_a_scan_becomes_selectable_text` (the fixture starts with no text layer and comes back containing "Scanned page" and "quick brown fox"). The Phase-4 gate: `test_convert_api.py::test_ocr_makes_the_text_readable_and_labels_the_version` asserts `is_scanned_page` flips to `false` and the editor gets blocks; e2e `phase-6.spec.ts::OCR a scan, then read, edit and export it` walks the whole path — the editor refuses the scan, its CTA opens the OCR panel, and afterwards the editor opens on the same document and the find bar locates a word only OCR could have added. `phase-4.spec.ts` was updated to the other half of its own criterion ("then enabled").
- [~] All six export formats + four import routes produce valid outputs from the UI. → **Every format and route is proven; two of the six are driven *from the UI*.** Word (workspace `export-docx` + `/pdf-to-word`) and images (`/pdf-to-jpg`) go through the browser in e2e; `txt`, `md`, `html` and `pdfa` are proven at engine and API level only, because six e2e round trips of the same button would buy coverage of the button, not of the formats. Recorded rather than ticked as written. → **Exports**: `test_convert.py` asserts each one on its own terms — Word opens via **python-docx** and contains the fixture text, images is a zip with one entry per page (and honours DPI), text has a separator per page, Markdown and HTML carry the text (HTML stitched into *one* document, not three), PDF/A probes clean and carries the `pdfaid` XMP marker. API level: `test_convert_api.py::test_every_cheap_export_downloads` (parametrized over four) + `::test_pdfa_export_claims_conformance` + `::test_the_image_export_zip_has_one_entry_per_page`. **Imports**: office (`::test_a_docx_becomes_a_readable_pdf`, against the real Gotenberg), images incl. multi-frame TIFF (`::test_a_multipage_tiff_keeps_all_its_frames`), HTML (`::test_an_html_file_becomes_a_pdf`) and URL (`::test_url_conversion_refuses_the_private_space` and `::test_gotenberg_itself_refuses_the_private_space`, the latter against the running container). ⚠ The URL→PDF *success* path is not tested: it needs a public address reachable from the container, and a test that depends on the internet is a test that fails on a train. The refusals are what the acceptance criterion asks for; the success path is exercised by hand. From the UI: e2e `::importing a file that is not a PDF converts it` and the `/jpg-to-pdf` guest spec.
- [x] URL→PDF refuses `http://169.254.169.254/`, `http://localhost:8000/`, `file:///etc/passwd` with clear errors (tests prove). → `apps/core/tests/test_urlguard.py`, 31 tests: the three named addresses verbatim plus the rest of the private space (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, ::1, fd00::/8, 0.0.0.0, `127.1`), our own service names, cloud-metadata hosts by name and address, and four non-web schemes. `::test_the_refusal_says_why` asserts the message names the reason; `::test_a_name_that_resolves_into_the_private_space_is_refused` and `::test_one_private_answer_out_of_several_is_enough_to_refuse` cover the case the literal list misses. Enforced at both layers: `test_convert_api.py::test_url_conversion_refuses_the_private_space_before_a_job_exists` (400, and **no Job row**) and `test_convert.py::test_url_conversion_refuses_the_private_space` in the worker. e2e drives it through the dashboard's URL box.
- [x] Compare UI: synced scroll, clickable change list, obviously-correct results on the crafted pair. → The crafted pair is `compare-a.pdf`/`compare-b.pdf`, built by the committed generator with two kinds of difference (an added line on page 1, a stamped box on page 2); a *replaced* word is covered by `::test_a_replaced_word_is_reported_with_both_sides`, which synthesises its own pair. `test_compare.py`: `::test_the_crafted_pair_reports_the_injected_change` (the added line, with a rect on the side that has it), `::test_the_visual_pass_sees_a_change_the_text_diff_cannot` (page 2 has **no** text change and the stamped box is found where it actually is), `::test_a_replaced_word_is_reported_with_both_sides`, `::test_an_extra_page_is_a_change_not_a_silent_omission`, `::test_the_offset_aligns_pages_that_moved`, `::test_a_document_compared_with_itself_is_identical`. UI: `compare.facade.spec.ts` (**8** tests — *corrected 2026-08-22; cited as 9*) covers the flattening into a clickable list, the per-side rects, and selection; e2e `::compare two documents and click through the changes` clicks a row and both filters. Sync is by construction — a row carries its page pair, so selecting one moves *both* overlays.
- [x] Hebrew OCR: heb fixture produces RTL text correctly extractable (owner-relevant check). → `scanned-hebrew.pdf` added to the corpus by the committed generator (rendered through `insert_htmlbox` so the pixels are in reading order, rasterised at ~300 dpi because Hebrew is harder than Latin at 180). `test_ocr.py::test_hebrew_is_recognised_and_comes_back_readable` runs tesseract with `heb` and asserts real Hebrew letters come back, including the word שלום. `::test_the_worker_image_ships_the_five_language_packs` asks **tesseract** what is installed rather than trusting a constant.

### Phase 4 — Content Editing · ✅ Complete (2026-08-02)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-04-content-editing.md):
- [x] **Guest parity + tool pages (§20 DoD item 9, §21.6):** every tool in this phase works end-to-end with no account, and ships its public SSR page — `/edit-pdf` `/watermark-pdf` `/add-page-numbers` — with unique title/meta/H1 and an entry in the generated `sitemap.xml`. → Guest parity: `test_content_api.py::test_a_guest_edits_text_end_to_end`, `::test_a_guest_can_read_the_content_models`, and `::test_every_phase_4_op_is_reachable_by_a_guest` (structural — the default permission is `IsPrincipal`, so this catches an op that *becomes* account-only). e2e: `phase-4.spec.ts::a guest watermarks from the public tool page`, `::a guest adds page numbers`, `::/edit-pdf hands a guest straight into the editor` — each asserting `login-form` absent throughout. `npm run verify:prerender` → *"11 tool pages server-rendered with unique title/H1, FAQ + SoftwareApplication JSON-LD, canonical, sitemap and robots."*
- [x] Click-to-edit works on the text fixture with visually acceptable output (side-by-side pixel diff <15% in edited block region for same-text roundtrip sanity test). → `test_content.py::test_edit_text_replaces_a_block_and_leaves_its_neighbours` (new text present, old gone, **neighbouring block untouched**), `::test_edit_text_batches_two_blocks_on_one_page`, `::test_edit_text_unicode_and_rtl_survive` (Hebrew), `::test_edit_text_escapes_markup_instead_of_rendering_it`, `::test_edit_text_keeps_the_file_from_ballooning` (<1.5× on the 199 KB fixture). e2e edits a paragraph and then *searches the saved file* for the new text. ⚠ The literal "pixel diff <15%" is **not** the assertion used: a same-text round trip through redact-and-reinsert cannot be pixel-stable because the replacement font is a substitute by design (see the fidelity constraint below), so a pixel threshold would measure font substitution rather than edit quality. The properties that criterion is protecting — right text, neighbours intact, size sane, no markup injection — are each asserted directly. Recorded in the Decisions log rather than silently reinterpreted.
- [x] Scanned-page gate appears on scanned fixture with working OCR CTA (disabled until P6, tooltip "coming with OCR tool" — then enabled). → `test_content.py::test_a_scan_is_flagged_so_the_editor_can_refuse` and `test_content_api.py::test_text_blocks_flags_a_scan`; e2e `phase-4.spec.ts::the scanned-page gate blocks the editor and offers OCR` asserts the CTA is **disabled** and carries `title="coming with OCR tool"`. The `ocrAvailable` input is the single switch Phase 6 flips.
- [x] Find & replace with preview/deselect works across a 50-page doc in <60 s. → `test_content.py::test_find_replace_across_fifty_pages_is_quick` **measures** the elapsed time on a 50-page document (50 replacements). Preview/deselect: `::test_find_replace_honours_only` (unchecked matches survive), `::test_find_replace_dry_run_count_equals_executed_count`, API `test_content_api.py::test_find_replace_dry_run_makes_no_version` (a search mints no version) and `::test_find_replace_executes_only_the_kept_matches`; `edit.facade.spec.ts` covers the review state; e2e unticks 1 of 3 and applies 2.
- [x] All stamp tools produce correct output honoring range + skip-first. → `test_content.py`: `::test_page_numbers_number_every_page`, `::test_page_numbers_skip_first_and_restart` (cover unstamped, numbering restarts at 1 on page 2), `::test_page_numbers_honour_an_explicit_range`, `::test_header_footer_fills_the_slots_and_computes_tokens` ({page}/{total}/{date}), `::test_bates_is_continuous_and_reports_its_range` + `::test_bates_over_a_range_stays_continuous`, `::test_watermark_under_content_leaves_text_extractable`, `::test_watermark_tiled_repeats`, `::test_watermark_image_renders`, `::test_overlay_pdf_stamps_a_letterhead`, and `::test_stamps_land_correctly_on_a_rotated_page`.
- [x] Whiteout vs Redact distinction communicated in UI (copy reviewed). → `edit.html` `[data-test=whiteout-note]`: "Whiteout **hides** content visually — the text underneath is still in the file and can still be copied out. To remove it permanently, use **Redact** instead." Asserted by e2e `::whiteout states plainly that it is not redaction`, and the behaviour it describes is pinned by `test_content.py::test_whiteout_covers_the_pixels_but_leaves_the_text_extractable` (region blank, text still extractable). The same distinction is in the `/edit-pdf` page copy and its FAQ.

### Phase 3 — Annotations & Markup · ✅ Complete (2026-08-01)

**Acceptance criteria** (copied verbatim from phase-03-annotations.md):
- [~] All listed tools usable; annotations visible after download in an external viewer (manual check with Preview/Acrobat reader on the exported file). → **Machine half done; the manual half is the owner's.** All 16 palette tools ship (select, highlight, underline, strikeout, squiggly, note, text box, rect, ellipse, line, arrow, polygon, polyline, ink, stamp, custom image stamp, + crop). `test_annotations.py::test_every_annotation_type_round_trips` is parametrized over **14 types** and asserts each is present in `/Annots` **read back by pypdf**, not PyMuPDF — a second library confirming the first; `test_annotations_api.py::test_annotations_survive_a_download_and_reopen` does the same on the bytes that come out of `GET …/download/`. What no test can assert is "it *looks* right in Acrobat", which is what this criterion literally asks for, so it stays `[~]` and the five-minute check is filed in the Human review queue rather than ticked on its behalf.
- [x] Save of a 30-annotation session = ONE job, <5 s on default queue. → `test_annotations.py::test_a_batch_of_thirty_is_one_pass_and_well_under_five_seconds` (30 adds → one `apply_annotation_ops` call, 30 annots in the output) *(name corrected 2026-08-22 — it was cited truncated at `..._is_one_pass`, which matches nothing)*; `annotations.facade.spec.ts::sends the whole session as ONE annotate_batch job` asserts exactly one `POST …/operations/` carrying 30 ops. Whole 51-test golden module runs in **0.85 s**, so a 30-op batch is far inside the 5 s budget.
- [x] Comments sidebar navigates and edits; authorship shows display name (or "Guest"). → **navigation**: `e2e/tests/phase-3.spec.ts` moves to page 2, clicks `[data-test=comment-jump]` and asserts the viewer jumped back to page 1; **editing**: opens `[data-test=comment-edit]` on a *saved* comment, rewrites it, and asserts the new text survives a save; **deleting**: `[data-test=comment-delete]` drops the row count; **clear page vs clear all**: a separate spec proves clearing page 2 leaves page 1's marks alone. Timestamps render (`[data-test=comment-time]`), and someone else's comment shows its author instead of an Edit button (`isOwn`, backed by `test_editing_someone_elses_annotation_keeps_their_name`). Authorship: `test_annotations_api.py::test_author_is_the_display_name_for_an_account` ("Alice") and `::test_author_is_guest_for_a_guest_and_leaks_no_session_id` (asserts the raw guest token is **not** in the downloaded bytes). e2e checks "E2E Tester" after reload and "Guest" on the guest path.
- [x] Annotating works end-to-end as a guest, and `/annotate-pdf` ships as a public SSR tool page in the sitemap (§20 DoD item 9, §21.6). → `e2e/tests/phase-3.spec.ts::a guest annotates from the public tool page with no login prompt` (cold context → `/annotate-pdf` → upload → draw → save, asserting `login-form`/`register-form` absent throughout); API-level `test_annotations_api.py::test_a_guest_annotates_and_flattens_end_to_end`. `npm run verify:prerender` → *"8 tool pages server-rendered with unique title/H1, FAQ + SoftwareApplication JSON-LD, canonical, sitemap and robots."*; `tool-pages.spec.ts` + `seo-artifacts.spec.ts` enforce table↔routes↔sitemap parity.
- [x] Flatten produces a version where text markup is permanent and no annotation objects remain. → `test_annotations.py::test_flatten_bakes_markup_and_removes_the_objects`: annotation objects gone (extract `== []` **and** pypdf `/Annots` count 0) while the pixels in the marked region changed and the underlying text is intact. `test_annotations_api.py::test_flatten_keeps_the_marks_visible` re-renders the stored version and finds the red pixels. e2e asserts the sidebar empties and the version is labeled "Flattened annotations".
- [x] Autosave + unsaved-changes guard verified. → Three mechanisms, per phase-03 §2 ("autosave every 30 s / **on navigation**") and §"Save model UX" ("unsaved-changes guard on exit"): the 30 s interval, the `unsavedWorkGuard` `canDeactivate` route guard that commits pending marks when the user routes away, and the `beforeunload` guard for leaving the site. `dirty()` is composed from `ops()`, so the badge and both guards arm only when something would actually be sent (`annotations.facade.spec.ts::emits no op for deleting a draft that was never saved`). The dirty badge transition is asserted in e2e (`3 unsaved` → `All changes saved`). ⚠ Still **not** automated: the 30 s wall clock and the native `beforeunload` dialog — Playwright cannot assert a native unload prompt, and a 30 s wait is a flake generator. Recorded rather than papered over; the composer all three call is unit-tested.

Organize hands off to the workspace, exercised by the same e2e.

- [x] Guest isolation proven router-wide by tests (guest↛guest, guest↛user, user↛guest); expired token yields 410 `guest_expired`. → `apps/documents/tests/test_guest_isolation.py` — 7 tests over **all 12 per-document endpoints** for guest B, user, and an anonymous caller, plus job-scoping and list-scoping; `::test_expired_guest_gets_410_not_404_on_its_own_document`.
- [x] No ownership check outside `apps/core/principals.py` references `request.user` (proved by test, not by review). → `apps/core/tests/test_principals.py::test_no_ownership_check_outside_principals_module`, 12 tests. The gate covers **four** spellings (`request.user`, `job.user`, `owner=`, `context["request"].user`), tokenizes the source so it matches code not prose, and `test_allowlist_has_no_dead_entries` deletes exemptions that stop being load-bearing. `documents/tasks.py` and `documents/views.py` now contain **zero** matches.
- [x] Guest tier limits enforced at their boundaries; exceeding yields the standard error shape with copy that names the account upgrade. → `apps/core/tests/test_limits.py`, 19 tests: 25 MB upload → 413, storage quota at the exact boundary, `MAX_PAGES` at and over the limit, concurrency 1 (guest) vs 3 (free), hourly metered cap at 5. Each asserts `"account" in message.lower()`.
- [x] `guest_purge` hard-deletes an expired session's rows *and* its storage blobs; nothing orphaned. → `apps/core/tests/test_guest_purge.py`, 6 tests. `::test_purge_deletes_rows_and_blobs` forces a thumbnail render first (thumbnails are keyed `thumbs/{doc}/{seq}/p{n}@{w}.png`, so the rows cannot name them) and asserts the storage prefix is empty afterwards. Also proves live sessions and account documents are untouched.
- [x] Register-from-guest claims every document in one transaction; over-quota claim is refused whole with an itemized message. → `apps/core/tests/test_claim.py`, 14 tests: documents **+ jobs + usage counters** move, counters are summed, storage folds once, idempotent, `quota_exceeded` carries `would_transfer` + `overflow_bytes` + itemized `items[]` and leaves everything guest-owned. End to end in `e2e/tests/phase-2b.spec.ts` (claim banner + 3 documents in the new library).
- [x] **no code path lets an anonymous party choose a recipient address.** → `test_guest_abuse.py::test_no_guest_reachable_route_accepts_a_recipient_address` + `::test_guest_flows_send_no_mail` and `test_claim.py::test_no_email_is_sent_on_any_guest_path` (`mail.outbox == []`). **Closed by Phase 8 (2026-08-02):** the tripwire was written to fail loudly the moment such a route appeared, it fired on Phase 8's first full run, and it now resolves every `sign-request` route to its view and asserts `IsAccount`. The positive half is demonstrated by `esign/tests/test_sign_api.py::test_a_guest_cannot_send_a_signature_request` — 403 `account_required`, signup copy, and **no row created**.
- [x] Guest `self_sign` with `signature_upload_ref`. **Deferred to Phase 8 in 2B and delivered there (2026-08-02):** `esign/tests/test_self_sign.py::test_a_guest_signs_a_document_with_no_account` runs the whole path with no account (upload ref → op → `Signed (self)` version → the image is on the page and the date is stamped), and e2e `phase-8.spec.ts::a guest signs a PDF from /sign-pdf with no login anywhere` does it from the public tool page. 2B's reasoning held: the op is guest-reachable *by construction*, because `IsPrincipal` is the default and it never declared `IsAccount`.
- [x] `MAX_PAGES` is enforced at ingest for both tiers (it was not enforced at all before this phase). → `test_limits.py::test_max_pages_is_enforced_at_ingest_for_guests` / `..._for_accounts` / `test_max_pages_boundary_accepts_exactly_the_limit`. *(Third name corrected 2026-08-22: the `...` shorthand expanded to `test_max_pages_is_enforced_at_ingest_boundary_…`, which does not exist — the boundary case drops `is_enforced_at_ingest`.)* Enforced in `documents/services.py::ingest_pdf`, the first point where the page count is known.

See Decisions log.
- [x] Existing Phase 0–2 acceptance criteria still pass for authenticated users (no regression from the refactor), **except the two listed as superseded under Tests above**, which are rewritten in this phase rather than re-ticked as written. → Full suite green: **backend 232, frontend 40, e2e 7**. `e2e/tests/phase-1.spec.ts` and `phase-2.spec.ts` pass unchanged. The two superseded items are rewritten deliberately: `test_core.py::test_anonymous_read_is_not_an_auth_error` (+ `::test_error_shape_on_expired_guest_token`) and `e2e/tests/phase-0.spec.ts` (+ a new `/app/doc/:id` non-gating test). Authenticated-path regression coverage: `test_guest_worker.py::test_account_worker_paths_still_behave`, `test_limits.py::test_account_concurrency_limit_is_three`, `::test_concurrency_counts_only_the_callers_own_jobs`.
