## Decisions log

| Date | Decision | Rationale | Plan doc amended? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-07-19 | Keep `python:3.14-slim`; no fallback to 3.13. | Full engine stack (PyMuPDF 1.28.0 cp310-abi3, pikepdf 10.10.0 cp314-abi3, ocrmypdf, pyHanko, reportlab, pdf2docx, pymupdf4llm) installs & imports cleanly on 3.14.6. Risk in phase-00 retired. | No |
| 2026-07-19 | `pymupdf4llm` pinned **1.28.0** (arch §2 said "latest"; draft req had non-existent 0.0.31). | Package realigned versioning to track PyMuPDF; 0.0.x line stops at 0.0.27, current tracks 1.28.0. P6 dep, installed now per scaffold item 5. | No (arch said "latest") |
| 2026-07-19 | `reportlab` pinned **4.4.4** not 5.0.x. | 5.0 is a brand-new major (arch flagged ⚠ "check changelog"); 4.4.4 is the stable line and only needed for P8 certificate PDFs. Revisit at P8. | No (arch flagged for check) |
| 2026-07-19 | simplejwt token blacklist app enabled (`ROTATE_REFRESH_TOKENS`+`BLACKLIST_AFTER_ROTATION`). | Matches §17 "rotate+blacklist" and enables real logout. | No |
| 2026-07-19 | Hermetic `config.settings.test` (filesystem storage + eager Celery + locmem email). | Golden/API tests must not require the storage/redis containers; keeps CI coverage gates runnable. Engine `storage.py` gains a filesystem backend alongside S3. | No (implementation detail) |
| 2026-07-19 | Added `Document.metadata` JSON field, populated at ingest. | Info popover (P1) needs author/subject/dates without re-downloading the blob per request. §9 permits field additions by amending the doc. | **Yes — arch §9 Document line amended.** |
| 2026-07-19 | Host ports parameterized in compose (`API_PORT`/`WEB_PORT`/`DB_PORT`/`MAILPIT_PORT`/`STORAGE_PORT`), defaults = plan values (8000/4200/5432/8025/8333). **This machine overrides API_PORT=8010, DB_PORT=15432** in `.env`. | Host already runs an unrelated Django app on :8000 and Postgres on :5432/:5433. A clean machine still gets 8000/4200 by default. Frontend→api path is internal (`api:8000`), unaffected. | No (env-only; .env.example documents the knobs). |
| 2026-07-19 | Postgres volume mounts at `/var/lib/postgresql` (not `/var/lib/postgresql/data`). | postgres:18 image rejects the old `/data` mount (new version-subdir layout); container exited(1) until remounted. | No (compose fix). |
| 2026-07-19 | Dropped `--poll 2000` from the web dev-server command. | On Docker Desktop (Mac) the poll watcher fires spurious rebuilds on the virtiofs mount that deadlock the esbuild+Tailwind-v4 pipeline (container OOM/exit 137). Without poll the server compiles once and serves stably; hot-reload is a Mac-only dev nicety, not required for e2e/acceptance. `ng build`/`ng serve` both hit the same deadlock only on *rebuild*, so a one-shot serve is unaffected. | No (compose fix). |
| 2026-07-19 | Upload "needs repair" signal = **damaged trailer/xref** (missing `startxref…%%EOF`), not just `pikepdf.open` failure. | Empirically qpdf AND mupdf silently reconstruct any recoverable PDF, so `pikepdf.open` only raises on truly-unrepairable files (fitz can't fix those either) — making the phase-01 "repair retry succeeds" flow untestable as literally worded. Trailer-terminator damage is the reliable "recovered-but-damaged" signal; repair re-saves a clean copy. Hard pikepdf failure that fitz *can* still open also → needs_repair. Implements the criterion's intent. | Phase-01 intent preserved; mechanism documented here. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Access model changed to anonymous-first.** No login required for any stateless tool; accounts become an upgrade. New Phase 2B inserted before Phase 3; 01-architecture gains normative §21. | The monetization model is advertising, and ad revenue scales with sessions — a login wall in front of "merge PDF" is a direct tax on the only revenue stream, and it gates precisely the highest-traffic, least-stateful operations. Every competitor that wins this category's search traffic (iLovePDF, Smallpdf, PDF24, Stirling, Sejda) is ungated for basic ops. Gated + ad-supported is the one combination that gets neither the volume ads need nor the focus subscriptions need. | **Yes — 01-architecture §§1, 6, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 16, 17, 19, 20 amended; §21 added (normative). README, 02-feature-matrix, phase-01/08/09/10 amended; phase-02b created.** |
| 2026-07-31 | Phase inserted as **2B**, not renumbered 3–11. | PROGRESS.md, both agent prompt files, the README index and every phase doc cross-reference phases by number. Renumbering would invalidate all of them for zero benefit. | No (numbering convention). |
| 2026-07-31 | Guest identity = **`X-Guest-Token` header in localStorage**, not a cookie. | Matches the existing JWT-in-localStorage interceptor pattern, keeps the API stateless and CSRF-free, and avoids a cookie-consent prompt on the exact page whose job is converting a first-time visitor in seconds. Tradeoff: clearing site data loses in-flight guest docs — acceptable because guest docs are ephemeral by design. | Yes — §21.2. |
| 2026-07-31 | Ownership funnelled through a single module (`apps/core/principals.py`), enforced by a **grep-based test** that fails if any ownership check outside it references `request.user`. | The refactor touches nearly every queryset in `documents`/`jobs`; a missed call site is a silent cross-tenant data leak, which is exactly the class of bug that review misses and a mechanical test catches. | Yes — §21.2, phase-02b acceptance. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Sending** sign requests stays account-only; self-sign is guest-accessible. | ESIGN/UETA needs an identified sender for attribution, and a guest-triggerable outbound email path is a spam relay pointed at our own domain reputation. Self-sign has neither problem and is a high-volume search term. | Yes — §21.3, phase-08 banner. |
| 2026-07-31 | Core abuse controls + per-tool public pages **moved out of Phase 9** into 2B / into each phase. | Both stop being end-of-project polish the moment the login wall comes down: throttling/TTL/Turnstile become load-bearing immediately, and SEO tool pages are the acquisition channel the ad model depends on, so they must ship with the tool rather than months later. | Yes — phase-09 rescoped, §20 DoD item 9 added. |
| 2026-07-31 | Guest retention: **24 h sliding from last access, 72 h hard cap**, hourly `guest_purge` hard-delete (no trash). | Long enough not to lose work mid-task, short enough to bound anonymous storage cost and breach blast radius. Also a marketing line ("no account, files auto-deleted within 24 hours") rather than a hidden limitation. `GUEST_TTL_HOURS` is the tuning knob if real usage disagrees. | Yes — §21.4. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Paid tier defined but not purchasable in v1** (`pro` row in §16, `User.plan` admin-only). No billing code, no checkout, no upgrade UI. | Honours the locked no-billing decision while forcing every quota check through `for_principal()` now — so adding billing later is "write a plan field + a webhook", not a refactor of every call site. Recorded alongside it: if billing is ever added, sell utility (limits, priority, batch, API), not ad-removal alone, which converts poorly against free ad blockers. | Yes — §16, §21.7. |
| 2026-07-31 | `METERED_OPS` = {ocr, convert_from, convert_to, compare} defined as a set **distinct from the `heavy` Celery queue**. | The queue also holds merge, alternate_mix, compress and repair — the flagship tool pages. Deriving guest rate caps or the Turnstile challenge from `op.queue` (the only machine-readable grouping that existed) would CAPTCHA a guest's first merge, defeating the anonymous-first strategy on its highest-traffic page. Queue membership is worker sizing; metering is cost control; they are deliberately different sets. | Yes — §16, §17, phase-02b. |
| 2026-07-31 | Short-window quotas (per-hour/per-day) are **Redis scoped-throttle counters**, not `UsageCounter` rows. | `core.UsageCounter` is month-granular by design (§9) and `usage_recompute` runs daily, so it cannot express "5 metered ops/hour". Accepted consequence: a Redis flush resets in-flight rate windows (Redis is also the broker, so a flush is already disruptive); monthly quotas survive in Postgres. | Yes — §16. |
| 2026-07-31 | Guest self-sign uses a new `signature_upload_ref` param on `self_sign`, not `signature_id`. | `signature_id` resolves to `esign.SavedSignature`, which is account-only — so as originally written the promised guest `/sign-pdf` path could not call the op at all. Ephemeral signature blobs live at `sigs/guest/{session}/{ref}.png` and purge with the session. | Yes — §10 registry, §13 keys, §21.3, phase-08. |
| 2026-07-31 | `ip_hash` salt rotation needs **no** version column, but must not rotate faster than `GUEST_TTL_MAX_HOURS` (72 h). | Sessions expire within 72 h, so a slower-than-72 h rotation only ever touches hashes that have already aged out. Rotating faster silently voids the IP leg of the throttle key for live sessions — the one thing it exists to do. | Yes — §17. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Two acceptance criteria amended to "verified in Phase 8"**: guest `self_sign` with `signature_upload_ref`, and the positive half of "sign-request creation as a guest returns `account_required`". | Neither feature exists — `esign` has no models and `self_sign` is not in the operation registry. Both criteria came from §21.3's *access-model* statement, and phase-02b's own Backend/Frontend scope sections contain no esign work at all, so the phase contradicted itself. Building them here would be importing a slice of Phase 8. What 2B genuinely delivers is the enforcement: `IsPrincipal` is the **default** permission, so a Phase-8 `self_sign` op is guest-reachable unless it declares `IsAccount`; and `test_no_guest_reachable_route_accepts_a_recipient_address` fails the build if a recipient-address route ever appears. Ticking them as written would have been a false tick. | **Yes — phase-02b acceptance criteria annotated.** |
| 2026-08-01 | Grep gate widened to **four** spellings and made token-aware: `request.user`, `job.user`, `owner=`, **`context["request"].user`**. | The plan named three. The fourth is how DRF serializers spell it, and blanking string literals (needed so the gate matches code rather than its own explanatory prose) makes `self.context["request"].user` invisible to all three named patterns — a real hole. Also added `test_allowlist_has_no_dead_entries`: the first version of the allowlist already contained 5 stale exemptions, and a stale exemption is a hole waiting for the next call site. | No (strengthens §21.2's gate). |
| 2026-08-01 | `created_by_user(job)` added to `principals.py` rather than exempting `documents/tasks.py` from the `job.user` pattern. | The worker stamps `DocumentVersion.created_by` from `job.user` — provenance, not ownership, and correctly `None` for a guest (§9). But exempting the file would have disabled the gate on **the exact module trap 1 lives in**. Routing the one legitimate read through the choke point keeps `documents/tasks.py` at zero matches, so the gate stays a clean signal. | No (§21.2 already says never read `job.user` directly). |
| 2026-08-01 | Claim pre-flight measures **`session.storage_bytes_used`**, not the sum of `Document.size_bytes`. | Caught by a failing test. The claim folds the session's storage counter into the user's, and that counter covers *every version blob*, while summing document sizes sees only current versions. A guest session with edit history could therefore pass pre-flight and still push the account over quota — the exact "never partially claim" failure §21.5 forbids. Both figures are now reported (`bytes`, `current_version_bytes`). | No (implements §21.5 correctly). |
| 2026-08-01 | An over-quota claim on **register/login** annotates the response with `claim_error` instead of failing the request; `POST /api/guest/claim/` still returns the hard 429. | §21.5 says an over-quota claim responds `quota_exceeded` and never partially claims. Applied literally to the inline path it would fail the *authentication* — leaving a just-created account unusable, or locking an existing user out, over a *guest* session. Nothing is transferred either way (claim is all-or-nothing) and the client keeps its guest token, so the files stay reachable and the user can free space and retry explicitly. The explicit endpoint preserves the specified behaviour. | No (interpretation of §21.5 for the inline path). |
| 2026-08-01 | `GuestFacade.ensureSession()` mints via `POST /api/guest/session/` **before** parallel uploads. | Found by e2e, not review. Lazy minting is per-request, so two concurrent tokenless writes each mint a session: the two files a guest drops on `/merge-pdf` landed in *different* sessions and the merge saw only one — breaking the flagship page this phase exists to ship. §21.2 already provides the explicit mint endpoint for exactly this. | No (uses §21.2 as intended). |
| 2026-08-01 | SSR ships as **`outputMode: "static"` (build-time prerender)**, not a Node SSR server. | Prerendering the landing + 7 tool pages produces real HTML that nginx serves exactly as it serves the SPA today — no new service, no change to the prod topology, and `/app/**` keeps its current SPA-fallback behaviour. Accepted consequence: `ng serve` returns the SPA shell, so "is it server-rendered?" must be asked of the build output; `npm run verify:prerender` asks it mechanically. Validating the SSR split early was a Human-review item — done, and the viewer was never server-rendered. | No (§7 only requires the tool pages be SSR/prerendered). |
| 2026-08-01 | Django `CACHES` pointed at Redis (db 1); test settings keep LocMem. | §16 says the short windows are "Redis scoped-throttle counters", but no `CACHES` was configured, so DRF throttles and the new metered-op windows were using the per-process LocMem default — meaning each API worker enforced its own private rate limit. A separate Redis db keeps them off the broker's keyspace. | No (implements §16 as written). |
| 2026-08-01 | Dev-only `THROTTLE_AUTH=60/min` and `THROTTLE_GUEST=240/min` in `infra/.env*`. | Both limits are per-IP and correct at §16's values; the whole e2e suite drives 7 specs and many guest sessions from one address inside a minute, which is precisely the pattern the IP legs exist to catch. Relaxing them in the dev env file — already-parameterised settings, no code change — keeps the production defaults intact (prod sets neither). Recorded rather than silently widened. | No (env-only; `.env.example` documents both). |
| 2026-08-01 | Tool-page copy is ~220–290 words, not exactly 300. | §21.6 asks for "~300 words of **honest** task copy". Padding to hit a number would make it worse, and thin filler is what search engines discount anyway. The test floor is 200 words with the reasoning written next to it. | No (within "~300"). |
| 2026-08-01 | `mypy` left at 98 errors (main has 74); **not** fixed in this phase. | `django-stubs` is installed but its mypy plugin was never enabled in `pyproject.toml`, so every model field in the project reports "Need type annotation" — my new fields simply add more instances of a pre-existing, project-wide condition. Enabling the plugin is a repo-wide change with unpredictable fallout and no relation to anonymous access. One genuinely new error of mine (an invalid `timezone.datetime` annotation) was fixed. §20's "mypy (loose) clean" has never held here; recorded rather than papered over. Suggested owner: Phase 10 hardening. | No — flagged for Phase 10. |
| 2026-08-01 | `eslint` not run: **no lint target exists in this repo.** | phase-00 listed `ng add @angular/eslint` but it never landed — there is no eslint config and no `lint` architect target, so §20's "eslint clean" has been vacuously true since Phase 0. Adding and configuring eslint mid-phase would surface a wave of findings across pre-existing code that this phase does not own. | No — flagged for Phase 10. |
| 2026-08-02 | **PyMuPDF's coordinate space differs per API — measured, and named at every call site.** | Phase 3 established that annotations are written in unrotated space. Phase 4 found the family is not even internally consistent: `insert_htmlbox` **does** apply the page rotation, while `draw_rect`, `insert_image`, `add_redact_annot`, `insert_link` and `show_pdf_page` do not, and `search_for` answers in display space while `get_text` answers unrotated. Mixing them puts content a quarter turn away on any rotated page. `content.py` therefore has `_display_rect` and `_raw_rect` rather than one converter, and `test_stamps_land_correctly_on_a_rotated_page` checks the *rendered* result. | **Yes — 01-architecture §8 carries the per-API table.** |
| 2026-08-02 | Acceptance criterion 2's "pixel diff <15%" is **not** the assertion used; the properties it protects are asserted directly instead. | A same-text round trip through redact-and-reinsert cannot be pixel-stable: the original font is almost always a subset that physically cannot render the replacement, so the replacement font is a substitute *by design* (a documented constraint of this phase). A pixel threshold would therefore measure font substitution, not edit quality — it would fail on a correct edit and pass on a wrong one in the same document. What the criterion is really protecting is asserted per property: the new text is present and the old gone, neighbouring blocks and other pages are untouched, unicode/RTL survive, markup is escaped rather than rendered, and the file does not balloon. | No (criterion's intent implemented; measurement changed, with reason). |
| 2026-08-02 | `search_for` has **no case-sensitivity option**; `match_case` is enforced by re-reading the text under each hit. | PyMuPDF's search is unconditionally case-insensitive, so the obvious `flags=` approach does not exist. Filtering hits against `get_textbox` of the same rect is the only way to honour the parameter, and it is cheap because it only runs for hits that already matched. | No (implements §10's `match_case` as specified). |
| 2026-08-02 | Inserted text is shaped into **ligatures**, and the search compensates. | `insert_htmlbox` shapes text, so editing a block to say "different" stores `di<ﬀ>erent` — and `search_for("different")` then finds nothing, meaning a user could not find the words they had just typed. Rather than degrade typography (only a monospace family avoids it), `_search` also looks for the ligature spellings. This is not only about our own output: real-world PDFs from LaTeX and InDesign are full of ligatures, so find & replace was previously broken on them too. The constraint itself is documented in the `/edit-pdf` copy. | No (fidelity constraint documented; mitigation recorded). |
| 2026-08-01 | `/annotate-pdf` **hands off into the workspace** (`?mode=annotate`) rather than running the tool inline on the page. | §21.6 says a tool page must *be* the working tool. For a one-shot op (merge, compress) that means run-and-download inline. Marking a document up is not one-shot: it needs a page raster, a text layer, a palette and a comments sidebar, and inlining that would mean shipping the workspace twice. The Phase-2B `/organize-pdf` page set the precedent for the interactive case. What §21.6 actually protects — no login prompt anywhere in the path, result downloadable, no gate — holds end to end, and `e2e/tests/phase-3.spec.ts::a guest annotates from the public tool page` asserts it as a cold guest. Recorded because the conformance review was right that it was previously undeclared. | No (interpretation of §21.6 for interactive tools). |
| 2026-08-01 | **§8 corrected: display space ≠ annotation space on a rotated page.** The engine now de-rotates on the way in and re-rotates on the way out. | §8 claimed PyMuPDF's page space is "also rotation-applied" without qualification. True for `page.rect`/`search_for`; false for `get_text("words")`, `annot.rect` and every `add_*_annot` — all of which are the page's *unrotated* space. On a /Rotate 90 page that is a quarter turn: a mark placed top-left was written top-right, and the overlay's word grid sat where no text was, making text markup attach to nothing. The trap is that it is invisible to a round-trip — write and read cancelled out, so the original `test_quads_are_correct_on_a_rotated_page` passed while both directions were wrong. Caught by the correctness lens of the self-review; now pinned by rendered-pixel assertions. | **Yes — 01-architecture §8 amended with the per-API table.** |
| 2026-08-01 | Client-supplied `author` on an annotation is **ignored**; an update preserves the existing annotation's author. | The field has to be *accepted* so an extracted annotation round-trips through an edit, but honouring it made §3's "display name, or Guest" a client-side claim: a guest could stamp "Jane Doe, General Counsel" into a file. Preserving the existing title on update is also simply correct — editing someone's comment does not make it yours (Acrobat does the same). Found by the security lens of the self-review. | No (implements §3 as written). |
| 2026-08-01 | Image uploads are rejected on **header dimensions**, before any decode, and are metered against the storage quota. | Measured: a 1.5 MB all-zeros PNG decodes to 40000×40000 ≈ 2.9 GB — inside every byte cap. The pixel check ran *after* `fitz.Pixmap(data)`, in the API process, where §12's worker memory limits do not apply and an OOM kill leaves no exception to catch. Separately, normalization re-encodes to lossless PNG (3–5× the JPEG that arrived) into a namespace no quota saw. Also: `throttle_classes` had *replaced* the defaults, silently dropping the guest token/IP throttles on a guest-reachable write — now added instead. Found by the security lens. | No (implements §16/§17 as written). |
| 2026-08-01 | Claim-on-signup **moves** `uploads/…` assets from the guest prefix to the account's. | §13's "blob keys are principal-independent, so claiming is metadata-only" holds for `docs/` and `thumbs/` but not for the new `uploads/{g\|u}/{principal}/…`. Left behind, a guest's stamps sat under a prefix the claim had just marked for expiry — `guest_purge` would delete them within the hour, at exactly the moment the funnel is selling persistence. Copy-then-delete for a handful of small objects is the cheap fix; keying assets by document instead would mean inventing a document for a signature image. | No (restores §21.5's "never silently drop"). |
| 2026-08-01 | The overlay's page raster is requested with `?annots=false`. | The overlay draws every annotation itself as editable SVG. A raster that already contained them showed each one twice, and the baked-in copy did not move when the editable one was dragged. The flag is part of the thumbnail cache key so the two variants cannot collide. | No (consequence of the own-raster decision). |
| 2026-08-01 | **Custom image stamps use a hand-built appearance stream on a real Stamp annot** — the "flattened stamp" fallback is not used. | phase-03 speced both paths and said "the golden test decides on day one". It did: `test_image_stamp_renders_and_round_trips` shows the appearance-stream route renders the pixels *and* keeps a real, addressable annotation that pypdf sees and that `extract` can round-trip back to `image_stamp`. The fallback (draw into page content, track as a pseudo-annotation) would trade away interop and addressability for nothing. Mechanism: the PNG becomes an Image XObject, and the annot's `/AP /N` stream is rewritten to draw it. | No (implements the speced primary path). |
| 2026-08-01 | **An `annotate_batch` update is delete-then-add with the same `/NM`**, not a per-property mutator. | Per-type in-place mutation needs one branch per property per type (rect, colours, opacity, border, contents, quads, vertices, ink…) and still has to force an appearance rebuild afterwards. The client already sends the full spec — session batching means the draft *is* the source of truth — so re-adding from it is both shorter and uniformly correct. Creation date is carried across so "modified" stays honest. | No (implementation detail). |
| 2026-08-01 | **Text-markup quads come from our own server text layer (`GET …/text-words/`), not from PDF.js's text layer.** | phase-03 said quads come from "PDF.js's own text layer, matching what the user sees". Our overlay renders a server-rendered page raster rather than drawing on PDF.js's canvas (see next entry), so there is no PDF.js text layer to consume — and sourcing word rects from the *same PyMuPDF that applies the annotation* removes an entire class of coordinate drift instead of adding one. RTL is stronger this way, not weaker: each word carries its own rect and its own (block, line) index, so a Hebrew selection groups by visual line regardless of direction. Proven by `test_hebrew_words_are_extractable_and_highlightable` and `overlay-model.spec.ts::handles right-to-left runs`. | Phase-03 intent preserved; mechanism recorded here. |
| 2026-08-01 | **`PageOverlayComponent` renders its own page raster instead of anchoring to ngx-extended-pdf-viewer's canvas.** | The viewer owns its DOM and re-creates it on scroll and zoom, so an overlay anchored to it fights for layout on every frame and breaks whenever the viewer's internals change. Rendering the page ourselves makes the overlay's coordinate space exactly the element's box, which is what lets §8's normalized geometry hold with *no* conversion at all — and it is the same primitive phases 4/5/7/8 need. Accepted consequence: annotate mode shows one page at a time with its own zoom, and the thumbnail width cap rose from 1200 to 2000 px so placement is not guesswork on HiDPI. | No (§7 requires an overlay layer, not a particular host). |
| 2026-08-01 | Ephemeral **image assets** get their own key namespace `uploads/{g\|u}/{principal}/{ref}.png`, with a tier-resolved per-file cap and a dedicated throttle scope. | Custom stamps (P3), inserted images and image watermarks (P4) and guest signatures (P8) are not documents — no versions, no page count, no library entry — so reusing `docs/` would have meant fake Document rows. The ref is opaque and the key is **derived from the caller's principal**, so a leaked ref cannot address another principal's asset (`test_one_principal_cannot_stamp_another_principals_image`). Uploads are re-encoded to PNG, which normalizes the format for the engine and drops EXIF/GPS before it can be pasted into a file the user is about to share. Guest assets are swept by `guest_purge`. | **Yes — 01-architecture §6 URL map, §13 storage keys, §16 tier table amended.** |
| 2026-08-01 | Two MuPDF **segfault** traps, both found by tests rather than review: (a) creating or deleting a page invalidates every live `Page`/`Annot` handle; (b) `doc[i].annots()` lets the temporary `Page` be collected while its annots are still in use. | Getting an Image XObject into a PDF requires inserting it on a page, so the naive image-stamp implementation created a scratch page *while holding* the annot it was about to modify — a hard crash, not an exception. Fix: embed every image up front, before any annotation work, so no handle spans a page mutation. (b) is subtler: `annot.parent` is a **weak** reference in PyMuPDF, so the idiomatic-looking `for a in doc[i].annots()` reads freed memory. Both are now commented at the call site, because the next phase to touch annotations will otherwise rediscover them the same way. | No (implementation traps, documented in code). |
| 2026-08-02 | **Radio groups are built at the pikepdf level**, not with `fitz.PDF_WIDGET_TYPE_RADIOBUTTON`. | Measured, not assumed: PyMuPDF's radio widget raises "bad xref" on the first `add_widget` and "bad rect" on `update()`, and gives every kid the same `Yes` on-state — so the options cannot be told apart and the group can never be exclusive. A radio group is properly *one* parent field with N kid widgets, each with its own export value, which is what `_build_radio_group` writes: create the kids as checkboxes, then re-parent them and rename each kid's "on" appearance state to its option. `test_radio_options_are_mutually_exclusive` is the proof. | No (phase-05 speced "N widgets sharing a name"; mechanism recorded). |
| 2026-08-02 | The radio kids' temporary names use a **printable** separator (`␀zenradio␀`), not `\x00`. | The obvious NUL separator does not survive the round trip through PyMuPDF's string writer, so the pikepdf pass found no kids and silently produced two loose checkboxes instead of a group — a wrong result with no error anywhere. Found by a failing exclusivity test. | No (implementation trap, commented at the call site). |
| 2026-08-02 | Clearing a text field writes `/V` **through the xref** (`xref_set_key(widget.xref, "V", get_pdf_str(""))`), not through `widget.update()`. | `field_value = ""` followed by `update()` is a no-op in PyMuPDF: the empty string is treated as "nothing to write", so a user who deleted the contents of a field and saved got the old value back. That is the exact motion the import round-trip criterion performs ("export → **clear** → import"). | No (implementation trap). |
| 2026-08-02 | XFA detection uses the **path** lookup `xref_get_key(catalog, "AcroForm/XFA")`. | `/AcroForm` is an inline dictionary in some files and an indirect reference in others; resolving it by hand only handles the second, so half of all XFA documents went undetected — and an undetected XFA form is precisely the case the warning banner exists for. | No (implements phase-05's `is_xfa`). |
| 2026-08-02 | Signature fields go through **pyHanko** even though PyMuPDF can emit a `/Sig` field. | phase-05 states PyMuPDF cannot create them; measured, it can. pyHanko is used anyway because it is the library that will *consume* the field in Phase 8, and `SigFieldSpec` is where seed values and lock dictionaries live — things the Widget API cannot express. Recorded because the phase doc's stated reason turned out to be wrong while its instruction was still right. | Phase-05 mechanism kept; its rationale corrected here. |
| 2026-08-02 | `import_form_data` takes the file's **text inline** (≤ 2 MB), not a "file ref" as §10 said. | There is no generic file-upload endpoint — §13's `uploads/…` namespace is images only — so a ref would have meant inventing an upload path for a few kilobytes of JSON. Inline keeps the import on the same job pipeline as every other mutation. | **Yes — 01-architecture §10 `import_form_data` row amended.** |
| 2026-08-02 | The form read model reports a radio group's placements as **`widgets[]`**, where phase-05 named a `radio_group` field. | PyMuPDF reports the kid widgets, not the parent, so the model collapses same-named widgets into one field entry — at which point "which group is this field in" is answered by the entry itself and `radio_group` would always be its own name. `widgets[]` carries what the UI actually needs: one rect and one on-value per placement. `default` (`/DV`) and `align` (`/Q`) from the same spec **are** implemented; neither has a PyMuPDF Widget attribute, so both go through the xref. | Phase-05 field list otherwise implemented as written. |
| 2026-08-02 | Fill mode drives **PDF.js's own widgets** via `[(formData)]`; the overlay-inputs fallback was not needed for any field type. | phase-05 asks for a decision per field type. Measured in the browser: PDF.js renders text, checkbox, radio, combobox and listbox as real form controls keyed by field name (`element.name = data.fieldName`), and the `e2e` spec drives four of them directly — so every *fillable* type works on the primary path. Signature is the only type with nothing to fill, and it is a placeholder by design. The panel's per-field inputs remain as the keyboard/accessible route into the same facade state, not as a second source of truth. | No (the phase's own "decision per field type", recorded). |
| 2026-08-02 | The form **builder** uses our overlay, not the viewer. | Placing a field is a drawing gesture on a page raster we control; there is no rendered widget to interact with until the field exists. This is the split phase-05 describes, and it reuses the Phase-3 primitive unchanged. Consequence recorded: a radio group is drawn once and its remaining options are laid out below at the same size, because asking the user to draw N boxes of matching size by hand is a worse tool. | No. |
| 2026-08-02 | **`TokenService` now holds the access token in a signal.** | Found while building fill mode, and much larger than this phase: `GuestFacade.principal()` is a `computed` over `TokenService.isAuthenticated`, which was a plain localStorage getter — no signal, so the computed cached whatever it read the first time. Evaluated once on the login page, it stayed `null` for the rest of the session, and the workspace's `authHeaders()` therefore sent the PDF viewer's *own* fetch (which bypasses the HTTP interceptor by design, §21.2 trap 5) with no credential. Every document 404'd and the viewer rendered nothing, for accounts and guests alike, in **view mode too**. No test caught it because none asserted that a page actually renders. Now pinned by `guest.facade.spec.ts::notices a JWT that arrives after the principal was first read` and, end to end, by the Phase-5 fill spec. | No (bug fix). |
| 2026-08-02 | `registry.get_op(job.type)` moved **inside** the worker's try block. | Also found by the e2e run: a worker holding a stale registry raised before the handler, after `mark_running`, so the job sat in `running` for ever and the client polled something that would never reach a terminal state. Anything that throws between `mark_running` and the handler has the same shape. `test_operations.py::test_a_job_that_cannot_start_ends_failed_rather_than_running`. | No (implements §12's terminal-state contract). |
| 2026-08-02 | `?format=json\|csv` on the export endpoint pins DRF's content negotiation to the first renderer. | `format` is DRF's own renderer override, so `?format=csv` 404s during negotiation *before the view runs*, and `?format=json` only works by accident. The phase doc spells the parameter, so the endpoint keeps it and negotiation is neutralised for that view alone; errors still render as JSON. | No (implements phase-05's URL contract). |
| 2026-08-02 | Exports are a **new result kind** (`export`) rather than a version or a document. | A Word file, a zip of page images or a text dump is not a state the PDF could advance to, so minting a version for one would put "Exported txt" in a document's history and make `revert` offer to go back to a text file. They land at `exports/{job_id}/…` behind `GET /api/jobs/{id}/download/`, metered against storage like every other write, and swept by the §15 24 h TTL. | **Yes — §10 `produces` now names `export`.** |
| 2026-08-02 | `convert_from` is a **cross-document** op even though it touches no other document. | Its input is an uploaded file or a web address, so there is no primary document in the URL — which is exactly what `is_cross_document` means. It is also the only op that legitimately mints a guest session at operation time: a guest converting a URL may never have uploaded anything. | No (uses §11's existing split). |
| 2026-08-02 | Conversion **sources** reuse the `uploads/{g\|u}/{principal}/…` namespace, keeping their real extension. | A Word file waiting to be converted is exactly as ephemeral as a Phase-3 stamp: no version history, no library entry, and it should die with the guest session. Reusing the namespace inherits the ref rules (opaque, principal-derived, so it cannot address another principal's file), the storage metering and the `guest_purge` sweep for free. The extension is kept because LibreOffice decides what it is looking at from the filename. | **Yes — §13 `uploads/…` is no longer PNG-only.** |
| 2026-08-02 | The SSRF guard runs at **both** the API edge and in the worker. | The edge check turns a bad address into an immediate 400 instead of a job that fails a minute later; the worker check exists because params are stored on the Job row and a job can be redelivered. Layer 2 (Gotenberg's own deny-list, evaluated per navigation) is what covers redirects and DNS rebinding, which no up-front check can. | No (implements §17's three layers). |
| 2026-08-02 | `EngineError` gained a per-instance `code`. | phase-06's OCR failure taxonomy names six outcomes (`already_ocred`, `tagged_pdf`, `ocr_engine_error`, encrypted, unsupported, timeout). Three of them are one-off enough that a class each would be noise, and the client needs to tell them apart — `already_ocred` is what makes the UI tick "Redo OCR" for you. | No (extends §11's taxonomy mechanism). |
| 2026-08-02 | OCR runs **in-process** with `jobs=1`. | In-process because the Python API surfaces OCRmyPDF's exception types, which is what lets a failure become a sentence rather than "exit status 2". `jobs=1` because the heavy queue already runs jobs in parallel: letting each OCR fan out over every core turns two concurrent jobs into a machine that serves neither. | No (implements the phase's "engine runs `ocrmypdf.ocr` in-process"). |
| 2026-08-02 | An image is converted to a PDF *before* being drawn onto the page. | Found by a failing test: `show_pdf_page` refuses anything that is not a PDF, so the obvious `fitz.open(stream=png)` route raises "is no PDF". `Document.convert_to_pdf()` first also handles a multi-frame TIFF for free — every frame becomes a page, which is the case most image-to-PDF converters quietly get wrong. | No (implementation trap). |
| 2026-08-02 | Compare's visual pass samples an 8 px grid rather than every pixel. | A 150 dpi render of an A4 page is 1240×1750; a per-pixel union-find over that in Python is both slow and less useful than a handful of rectangles a human can click. A change invisible at 8 px is invisible to the reader too. Regions smaller than 0.02% of the page are dropped, because anti-aliasing and subpixel drift otherwise produce a scatter of meaningless boxes on pages that are in fact identical. | No (implements "pixel XOR heat regions, ignore <2% noise"). |
| 2026-08-02 | An unowned `other_document_id` fails the **job** rather than returning 404 at POST. | Consistency, not preference: `overlay_pdf` (phase 4) and `insert_from_document` (phase 2) have behaved this way since they shipped, and the worker resolves every source id through `owned_by(..., principal_of(job))`, so nothing of the other principal's document is read either way. Adding an edge check for compare alone would have made three sibling ops answer three different shapes. Recorded because a reviewer will reasonably ask. | No (matches the shipped contract). |
| 2026-08-02 | The seal names `subfilter=PADES` explicitly, and `/verify` requires **whole-file coverage** before it says "intact". | Two findings from the same review, both about a claim being wider than the code. pyHanko's default subfilter is the legacy Adobe profile, so the file was a valid CMS signature and *not* PAdES while the report written into the audit chain called it "B-B". And `status.intact` covers only the signed byte range: an incremental update appends a revision, leaves that range alone and changes what the document says — pyHanko calls that `ENTIRE_REVISION`, and the verifier was calling it intact. A forged contract with a green tick is the worst thing this product could produce. | Yes — §9's `event_hash` and §13's keys amended alongside |
| 2026-08-02 | `AuditEvent.save()` locks the parent row, and the chain is **HMAC-keyed**. | Two concurrent writes — two recipients in a parallel group, or two taps on Download — read the same tail and both wrote the same `prev_hash`, forking a chain that is append-only and therefore unrepairable: `verify_chain` would report broken for ever on a request where nothing wrong had happened. And an unkeyed chain is only self-*consistent*; the key lives in the environment, so rewriting history now needs the application's secret as well as its database. | Yes — §9 |
| 2026-08-02 | A document with a sign request **cannot be permanently deleted**. | `source_version` is `PROTECT`, so the delete failed anyway — but only after every blob had been removed from storage and the quota credited back, leaving rows pointing at files that no longer existed and a ceremony nobody could complete. Refusing up front, with a sentence explaining why, is the honest version. | Yes — §9 |
| 2026-08-02 | The ceremony shows the **page** with the fields positioned on it, served as a rendered image. | phase-08 asks for "doc viewer (read-only) … field widgets overlaid at §8 coords", and the first cut shipped a flat list of cards with a link out to the PDF — a signer who cannot see where their mark lands is signing blind. An image rather than a PDF viewer because §8 geometry maps onto a rendered page as plain CSS percentages, and shipping a PDF engine to a phone to display one page is not a trade worth making. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | Placed signatures are **de-rotated and counter-rotated** (§8). | Measured, not assumed: `insert_image` and `insert_textbox` write in the page's unrotated space, so a field placed at the displayed top-left of a landscape page landed off the right edge — and once positioned correctly, the image was drawn lying on its side. Both halves need a geometry assertion to catch, which is what §8 says about this exact class of bug. | No (§8 already states the rule) |
| 2026-08-02 | `fit_text` shrinks to fit rather than letting `insert_textbox` drop the text. | It returns a negative number and **writes nothing** when the text does not fit, so a date in a box somebody drew thin, or a tick in a small square, silently vanished from the finished document while the signer had been told it was filled in. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `finalize_sign_request` runs on the **heavy** queue and under the §11 Redis lock. | §12 puts it on `heavy`; `.delay()` was sending it to `default`, whose worker is killed at 60 s — burn, seal and certificate on a real envelope will exceed that. The lock closes the other half: the seal was being written to storage *before* the status was claimed, so two simultaneous completions could leave `final_sha256` describing a file nobody has. | No (restores §12) |
| 2026-08-02 | One `normalize_signature` for every route a signature image arrives by. | A photograph has no alpha channel, so trimming was a no-op on it and the **paper** was being stamped onto the contract — while the pad's own copy told the user the paper had been removed. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | The consent gate answers **403 `consent_required`**, not 400. | §8B says "everything else 403s until consented". It is a permission state the ceremony branches on to show the consent screen, not a malformed request. | No (restores §8B) |
| 2026-08-02 | The `/verify` response nests per-signature detail under `signatures[]`, and the signer roster needs the actual file. | §8C sketches a flat shape, which cannot describe a document carrying more than one signature. The roster gating is the security half: the envelope code is printed on every page and appears in every email about it, so on a code match alone a forwarded message was enough to ask us who signed what, when, and at which employer. | Yes — §8C shape noted |
| 2026-08-02 | The four cursive signature faces are **vendored in the repo**, and a typed signature is rendered server-side. | A signing ceremony that fetches a font from Google tells Google who is signing what, and when. The fonts are OFL-1.1, which permits bundling; `apps/pdf_engine/fonts/README.md` records each licence. Nothing on `/s/:token` makes a third-party request. | No (phase-08 says "fonts vendored in repo") |
| 2026-08-02 | The signature pad is a plain canvas, not `signature_pad`. | Sixty lines of pointer events do the same job with a mouse, a finger and a stylus, and a signing ceremony is the last place to add a third-party script. phase-08 names the library as a suggestion, not a requirement. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `AuditEvent.save()` refuses a second write, and `delete()` refuses at all. | phase-08 asks for append-only "enforced in model + DB permissions in prod". The model half is here and tested; the DB-permission half is a Phase-10 deployment concern. An audit trail that the application can edit is a claim, not evidence — and the tests prove the chain detects an edit made *around* the model, with a queryset `update()`. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | Ceremony text fields save on **blur**, not per keystroke. | Found by the e2e hitting the public throttle: a POST per character is a request storm on a phone connection *and* an audit event per letter typed, which would bury the events that matter in a chain meant to be read by a human. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | The interceptor sends **no credential** to `/public/sign/` or `/verify`. | The token in the URL is the whole capability. Attaching this browser's account or guest token would tie a stranger's signing session to it, and would mint a guest session for somebody who only came to sign a PDF. `credential-free.spec.ts` pins it. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | A `viewer` completes by opening the document; a `cc` never blocks completion. | phase-08 poses the question in its own test list ("viewer role auto-completes on view?") and answers it: a viewer must open the document, and opening *is* completion. A cc is not asked to do anything, so waiting for one would stall every envelope that has one. `test_routing.py` has both as named tests. | No (the phase decides it) |
| 2026-08-02 | `THROTTLE_PUBLIC_SIGN` gets dev-only headroom, like `THROTTLE_AUTH` in Phase 7. | 20/min is right for one stranger signing one document, and wrong for an e2e run that plays every signer from one address. Production keeps the configured rate. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | B-LT is detected, not claimed. | The seal is B-B, or B-T when `TSA_URL` is set. Long-term validation material needs a real CA chain with revocation endpoints; with a self-signed development certificate there is nothing to embed, and claiming the level anyway would be a lie told in a field name. `/verify` says "not from a trusted authority" rather than showing a green tick. | No (phase-08 says "config-detected") |
| 2026-08-02 | Sanitize's "hidden layers" **deletes** off-layer content rather than flattening it visible, which is what phase-07 literally specifies ("set visible state + drop OC"). | Flattening does the opposite of what the checklist item promises: the content the reader could not see gets printed on the page, and the report calls it removed. The user's next act is to send the file. The off groups' marked-content sections are cut out of the page streams first; `booby-trapped.pdf` gained a genuinely hidden layer so this is measured rather than assumed. | Yes — §10's `sanitize` row rewritten |
| 2026-08-02 | `only: []` means "none of these", not "no filter". | The falsy check conflated an empty review list with an absent one, so unticking every row redacted every row — the exact inversion of the instruction, on the operation that cannot be undone. §10's `redact` row now says so, because it is a wire contract. | Yes — §10 `only[]` documented |
| 2026-08-02 | The verification pass counts only what the user asked to have removed, on the pages that were in scope. | Re-running every pattern over every page reports the match the user deliberately kept as "still findable" — which is the phase's own flow (find three, untick one, apply two), and the UI shows it as a red failure on a correct redaction. Residue is now `found − kept`, per page and per matched text. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | The phone preset requires punctuation and refuses a four-digit first group; the card preset knows the lengths cards are issued in and retries a narrower span; the IBAN preset accepts the spaced form. | Measured against the fixture: the first phone pattern made every separator optional, which made it "any eight digits" — it matched the invoice number, the order number and the part number on the fixture's own *must survive* list. The card pattern missed a real card preceded by a row number. The IBAN pattern missed the way banks actually print one. A preset that eats innocent content is worse than one that misses. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | Pattern matching runs under a **20 s SIGALRM deadline**. | A user-supplied regex is arbitrary work: `(a+)+#` against forty characters is minutes of CPU, `redact` is deliberately *not* in `METERED_OPS` (§16), and the queue has two workers — one request per guest session would hold both until the 900 s hard kill. `re` has no timeout of its own. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | A redaction preview's matched text is swept from the job row after an hour. | The dry run answers with the text of every match so the user can choose, and that answer is stored. `Job.document` is `SET_NULL`, so deleting the document does not take the preview with it: the social security numbers outlived the document they came from. The rects and the count stay, so the history still records what happened. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `redact` runs on the **heavy** queue, not `default` where §12 listed it. | Redacting a long scan re-encodes every image the boxes touch and then re-extracts every page to verify — that does not fit a 60 s soft limit, and a legal disclosure bundle is the use case redaction exists for. Queue membership is worker sizing, not cost control: `METERED_OPS` is unchanged, so nothing about a guest's allowance moves with it. The other three security ops are cheap and stay on `default`. | Yes — §12 queue table amended |
| 2026-08-02 | **Editing an unlocked document removes its protection, and says so** — the flag flips, the version label reads "… (password removed)", and the banner warns before the act. | An engine works on decrypted bytes and returns a new file. Neither PDF password is recoverable from the other, so the original encryption cannot be put back faithfully; re-encrypting with the one password we were given would quietly make it *both*, which is a silent downgrade of exactly the thing being protected. Preserving the encryption dictionary needs qpdf's `--copy-encryption`, which pikepdf does not expose. Disclosed rather than hidden, and pinned by `test_editing_an_unlocked_document_says_the_password_came_off`. | No (phase-07 does not specify the resulting version's state) |
| 2026-08-02 | The session password is held in **`DocumentPasswords`** (a dependency-free service) and attached by `DocumentsService.operation()`, not by each facade. | An encrypted document refuses *every* operation without it, so rotating a page needs it as much as re-encrypting does. In memory only — not localStorage, not a cookie — and `security.facade.spec.ts` asserts that rather than describing it. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `document_password` is a **write-only sibling of `params`**, not a member of it. | It is a credential for the *document*, not an input to the operation. Inside `params` it would have to be added to all forty op schemas, each of which would then have to remember to ignore it — and `Job.SENSITIVE_PARAMS` could not redact it generically. | Yes — §10 notes the request-level field |
| 2026-08-02 | Wrong-password attempts are throttled **per document** (5/min), charged in the worker and refused at the view. | The thing being guessed at belongs to the document; an attacker with several sessions would otherwise get five tries each, which is not a limit. | 2026-08-02 | Sanitize ends with a **`garbage=4` compaction pass**, and a removed note takes its `/Popup` with it. | Unlinking an object is not deleting it: qpdf keeps orphans, so a comment removed from `/Annots` was still sitting in the file for anyone reading the bytes — and a note's `/Popup` holds a reference back to it. | 2026-08-02 | The redaction tests grep **decoded** content streams (`raw_text_bytes()`), not the raw file. | PyMuPDF writes text as hex strings inside deflated streams, so `b"secret" not in pdf_bytes` was true *before* redaction too — the criterion's "raw-bytes grep" was passing vacuously. | 2026-08-02 | The PII fixture gained a third email address. | The review list only earns its existence if a user can keep some matches and drop others, and two is not enough to demonstrate that — phase-07's own E2E says "review shows 3, uncheck 1, apply 2". Regenerated through the committed generator; four count assertions updated with it. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | A thumbnail of a locked document answers **423**, not 500. | `doc[page]` on a document that needs a password raises out of PyMuPDF, and the thumbnail strip asks for one of these per page — so every protected document produced a wall of 500s in the browser console. "Unlock it" is something the client can act on. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `THROTTLE_AUTH` is relaxed to 200/min in **`config.settings.dev` only**. | The throttle is per IP and protects against credential stuffing, which is a production concern; in dev the entire e2e suite is one IP, and 10/min meant the eleventh test to register an account failed for a reason unrelated to what it was testing. Production keeps the configured rate. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | `/pdf-to-word` and `/pdf-to-jpg` hand back a **file**; `/compare-pdf` hands off to the workspace. | §21.6 says a tool page must *be* the tool. | 2026-08-02 | **A radio option's label is decoded on the way out, and the selection is made at the PDF level.** | An option is stored as a PDF *name*, so pikepdf escapes anything not name-safe on write and PyMuPDF hands the raw token back: the read model advertised `Option#201` — which is the builder's own default text, "Option 1" — and *neither* spelling then selected anything, because `field_value` re-escapes whatever it is given (`a#2fb` → `a#232fb`). Every builder edit escaped the escape again. Names are decoded in `_decode_pdf_name`, and `_select_radio_options` writes the parent's `/V` and each kid's `/AS` through pikepdf, which is where a radio group's answer actually lives. Found by the correctness lens. | No (bug fix). |

Found by the correctness lens. | No (bug fix). |
| 2026-08-02 | The radio builder registers **one** field, and deleting a group removes its parent. | The `/AcroForm/Fields` filter matched kids by `/T` *after* the loop had already deleted `/T`, so every kid stayed registered as a top-level field as well as a `/Kids` entry; and `_delete_field` only removed widget annotations, so a group's parent survived with dangling `/Kids` — which meant update (delete-then-add) left two fields with the same name. | 2026-08-02 | The PDF-native conversion is box arithmetic in `geometry.py`, **not** `~page.transformation_matrix`. | Measured on PyMuPDF 1.28: that matrix carries the page origin only at rotation 0. | 2026-08-02 | The read model reports `font_size` and `align`, and the builder re-sends every property on a move. | An update is delete-then-**add** from the spec the client sends, so every property the spec omits is silently reset: dragging a field wiped its max length, alignment, font size and default. `specOf()` is now the single place a field becomes a spec. | No (bug fix). |
| 2026-08-02 | `fill_form`'s `values` and `import_form_data`'s `data` are capped; the unknown-name error is truncated; the read model stops at 2 000 fields. | Job params and error messages are stored on the Job row and echoed on every poll, and these were the only unbounded strings in the schema file: a guest could park 4 MB in one job and re-download it at the throttle limit (measured: 11 MB from one `GET /api/jobs/`), and 90 000 unknown names produced a 900 KB error message. | 2026-08-02 | Export CSV neutralises leading `= + - @` (CWE-1236). | The values come out of a PDF somebody else may have written, and Export CSV is one click from a victim opening it in Excel. JSON is not a spreadsheet and is left alone. Found by the security lens. | No. |

Found by the regression lens. | No (bug fix). |
| 2026-08-02 | **Fill mode is the default *tab*, not an automatic mode switch.** | phase-05 says "Fill mode (default when doc has form)". Switching the workspace into forms mode on open would need `has_form` at open time, which means either a per-document PDF parse on every open — the exact cost Phase 3 refused for annotations — or a stored flag that goes stale the moment `edit_form_fields_batch` adds a field. | 2026-08-02 | The XFA fixture is hand-built, not taken from a public sample. | phase-05 §Tests says "add one to corpus from a public sample". Built by the committed generator instead so the fixture is small, license-free and minimal — what the test needs is the `/XFA` key the detector looks for plus a real AcroForm field beside it. Accepted consequence, recorded rather than assumed: detection is proven against a synthetic file, not against an Acrobat-authored one. | No (mechanism recorded). |
| 2026-08-01 | `storage.list_prefix` / `delete_prefix` added to both storage backends. | `guest_purge` must leave nothing orphaned, and thumbnails are keyed `thumbs/{doc}/{seq}/p{n}@{w}.png` — the page/width combinations actually rendered are not recoverable from the DB rows, so deleting only each version's `storage_key` would leave every thumbnail in the bucket forever. | No (implements §21.4's "nothing orphaned"). |
| 2026-08-02 | `List-Unsubscribe` points at the **API**, and the token carries a **keyed hash** of the address rather than the address. | RFC 8058 one-click is a server-to-server POST from Gmail with no JavaScript in sight. The hash is the same reasoning as `users/verification.py` signing a user id: the link is printed in every footer and clicked from a browser, and a URL with an email in it lands in history, proxy logs and `Referer` headers. | **Yes — §9 `core.EmailSuppression`, §19.** |
| 2026-08-02 | A browser **GET** on the unsubscribe link asks first, and suppression is **reversible**. | The link travels with a forwarded signing invitation, and a mail-security sandbox that follows links would pull it unattended. | 2026-08-02 | A **suppressed invitation is not a notified recipient**. | `_send` discarded `mail.send`'s return value, so the recipient was marked `notified`, `last_notified_at` was stamped, and the audit chain recorded a `sent` event with their address — for a message that never left the building. | 2026-08-02 | The abuse report is recorded **without the reporter's request context**, and the response no longer returns the running count. | `record(..., request=request)` stores `client_ip` and the user agent, and the audit trail is readable by the request's **owner** — so reporting a spammer handed the spammer your address. | 2026-08-02 | `PublicSignReportView` goes **through** the base-class guard, not around it. | It resolved the token itself and skipped the draft/closed/expiry checks, so three reports could permanently kill a request that had been sent to nobody — `canceled_by_abuse` is terminal, and the owner's only route back was rebuilding from scratch while being emailed about "the people you sent it to". | No (uses the guard that already existed) |
| 2026-08-02 | `UploadThrottle` counts **writes only**. | DRF builds throttles in `initial()` for every method, so the 20/hour upload cap was spent by the dashboard *listing* documents — twenty reads is a few minutes of ordinary use, and the frontend swallowed the 429 as an empty library. Reproduced on the running stack before the fix. | No (implements §16's "per-account upload rate") |
| 2026-08-02 | `/api/verify/` keeps an **hourly ceiling** behind its per-minute burst, and both are added to the defaults rather than replacing them. | Assigning `throttle_classes` replaces `DEFAULT_THROTTLE_CLASSES`, which had quietly loosened the endpoint from an effective 60/hour to 600/hour — on the one route that takes an arbitrary PDF from an anonymous stranger and runs signature verification on it. | No (restores the shipped ceiling) |
| 2026-08-02 | Public signing gains a **200/day/token** cap. | §9B's matrix names it and it had no implementation. The IP throttle stops one machine flooding; only a per-token cap stops one leaked link being replayed all day from a hundred addresses. | No (implements §9B) |
| 2026-08-02 | The verification mail has a **120 s per-account cooldown**. | Registration never proves the address belongs to whoever typed it, and the resend endpoint had no cap beyond a per-IP auth throttle: 15 requests produced 15 messages to a stranger's inbox in seconds. | 2026-08-02 | **Registration sends the verification link.** | The gate it unlocks was otherwise unreachable from a cold start: nothing else in the product asked for the address to be confirmed, and the only UI was a settings banner the person had never visited. | 2026-08-02 | The consent region comes from the browser's **IANA timezone**, not `navigator.language`. | `en-US` is the most common language tag in the world, including on machines in Berlin, Paris and Amsterdam — so reading the country out of the locale told most EU visitors they were American and skipped the banner they are entitled to. `Europe/Berlin` tracks where the machine actually is. | **Yes — §19 notes the input.** |
| 2026-08-02 | **Declining consent means no ads at all**, not non-personalized ads — and outside the consent regions, where nothing is asked, ads load. | phase-09 asks for NPA on decline. The second half is the opposite error: gating on an explicit `granted` everywhere meant an ad-funded product that could never show an ad outside the EEA, because nothing ever asked. | No (deviation recorded; `docs/09-adsense-readiness.md` states it) |
| 2026-08-02 | Withdrawing consent **unloads the tag and reloads**. | Hiding the slots and pushing a `denied` Consent Mode update left the Google script loaded and running for the rest of the session. | 2026-08-02 | `/app/doc` is a **forbidden ad surface**. | phase-09's hard exclusions name the editor/viewer canvas and it was missing from the list — the one screen where somebody is mid-way through redacting a contract, and a misclick lands on an advertiser's site. | 2026-08-02 | `ads.txt` is **rendered by the API** and proxied to the site root. | The only thing in the file is the publisher id and that lives in the environment, so a file baked into the frontend image would need a rebuild to change — and, being static, could not tell the truth before the AdSense account exists. | No (implements "publisher id from env") |

The constant is now the single source for that copy, and the test that pins it to the sweepers' settings runs where both are reachable. | No (makes the existing claim true) |
| 2026-08-02 | The recipient cap is enforced at **draft** time, not only at send. | The builder allowed 25 and the send refused above 10, so somebody could add eleven people, lay out a field for each, and be refused twenty minutes later — the shape this same file argues against for `enforce_sign_requests`. | 2026-08-02 | A **completed signature envelope survives the account that sent it**: `SignRequest.owner` and `.document` are `SET_NULL`, and the sender is snapshotted as text at send. | §10.1 asks for account deletion that erases documents while retaining other parties' completed envelopes. | 2026-08-02 | The **audit chain is never rewritten**, including by account deletion. | It is append-only and hash-linked: editing one row breaks every hash after it, so "anonymise the audit rows" would destroy the very thing being retained. | 2026-08-02 | `/api/health/live` is **dependency-free**, and `/api/health/` reports queue depth and a worker heartbeat. | A liveness probe that checks Postgres restarts the API every time Postgres hiccups, turning one outage into two. | 2026-08-02 | The **request id is sanitised** before it is used. | It is honoured from an inbound header so a proxy's id stitches to ours, and it lands in every log line — which makes it attacker-controlled text in a log aggregator unless the non-alphanumerics come out first. | No |

| 2026-08-02 | A zip-based upload is checked against its **declared** uncompressed size before it is stored. | A `.docx` is a zip and its central directory states the unpacked size without unpacking anything — which is the only moment refusal is cheap. | 2026-08-02 | `text-slate-400` body text, and white on `emerald-600`, are **gone from the product**. | 2.86:1 and 3.65:1 against the 4.5:1 AA threshold — found by axe across every route, not by looking. | 2026-08-02 | Playwright keeps **zero retries**, and cross-browser is opt-in. | A test that passes on the second attempt is telling you something about the product; a retry count is how that stops being heard. | 2026-08-02 | ~~**Sentry covers the API and the workers, not Angular.**~~ **Superseded the same day** — see the `ErrorHandler` row below. | 2026-08-02 | **There is no `@full` tag**: "full" is the suite with no `--grep`. | A tag you must remember to add is a tag somebody forgets, and the failure mode is a spec that silently runs in neither suite. Running everything by default makes omission impossible. | No — deviation recorded |
| 2026-08-02 | PDF.js's toolbar is **excluded** from the axe scan. | `ngx-extended-pdf-viewer` ships PDF.js's editor buttons with unsupported ARIA attributes and `pdf-shy-button` elements missing required ones. Excluded, written down, and queued as an upstream report. | No — deviation recorded |
| 2026-08-02 | **The thumbnail rail is not virtualized. | 2026-08-02 | **No `@sentry/angular`. A ~50-line `ErrorHandler` reports to our own API instead.** | §10.4 names Angular, and today a client-side exception reaches nobody — verified, not assumed. What held: making the SDK safe here means `beforeBreadcrumb: () => null` *and* deleting `event.breadcrumbs`, because the default event carries a document title from the dashboard and a live signing token from `location.href` — and what survives that is exception, stack and route, which is what the fifty lines send. It also avoids a source-map upload to a third party and a processor the privacy policy does not name. | No — implements §10.4's named requirement by another route. |
| 2026-08-02 | **A redelivered job that is still `running` is failed, not re-run.** | `acks_late` + `reject_on_worker_lost` put the message back when a child is SIGKILLed, and the row is still `running` precisely because no handler ran. Re-running means handing a worker the same document that just killed one, for as long as it keeps killing them — which is §10.1's "queue drains" failing under exactly the attack fixture it names. The trade is one job failing without a retry against the lane surviving. | No (implements §12's terminal-state contract) |
| 2026-08-02 | **`THROTTLE_UPLOAD` is relaxed to 600/hour in `config.settings.dev` only.** | §9B's 20/hour is one person's day; the pagination e2e has to build a library of 62 documents and spends it in two seconds. Same shape and same written reason as the four relaxations already there. Consequence, recorded rather than hidden: no e2e exercises the shipped dev number any more — `test_ads_and_abuse.py::test_uploading_too_fast_is_refused_with_a_retry_after` still pins the cap, with an overridden rate, under `config.settings.test` (which imports `base`, not `dev`). | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **Seven `type: ignore`s, and `warn_unused_ignores = true`.** | They mark the places where the stub is wrong and the runtime was verified right — pikepdf's instruction unpacking, pyHanko's int-annotated float box, ocrmypdf's fixed-kwarg overload, DRF's metaclass-popped declared fields, Django's own custom-user pattern. Not a way to reduce the count: the alternative measured worse. Stopping at the 31 errors that "carry value" leaves the tool reporting 41 findings, which means nobody reads it, which means it never goes in `test.sh` — which is how it reached 98. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **Type-aware ESLint is deliberately not in this change.** | 198 findings, 129 of them one root cause (`HttpErrorResponse.error: any`) whose honest fix is a typed helper at ~28 call sites. Bundled with the accessibility fixes the diff would be unreviewable, and the a11y half is the half that matters. Queued with the reasoning. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **`100.64.0.0/10` and `192.88.99.0/24` are named explicitly in the SSRF deny-list.** | Found by an adversarial pass, and the shape is the lesson: carrier-grade NAT is neither `is_private` nor `is_global`, so all six flags missed it — and the deprecated 6to4 relay *is* `is_global`, so adding `not ip.is_global` would not have closed it either. A derived rule would have missed one or the other; the ranges are written out. Pre-existing, and the layer-2 Gotenberg deny-list did not cover them either. | No (strengthens §17) |
| 2026-08-02 | **`version_retention` is removed, not implemented — and the storage quota is enforced on the version write instead.** | The cap was published to every client by `/api/config/` and enforced by nothing, so something had to change. Pruning was the obvious reading and is the wrong one: **three** tool pages say in so many words that the version history keeps every step and you can revert to any of them — `/organize-pdf`, `/annotate-pdf` and `/redact-pdf` (`frontend/src/app/core/tool-pages.ts:274,311,848` — *re-verified 2026-08-22; the lines were cited as 270/307/844 and had drifted by four*) — and six promise recoverability in some form. `/redact-pdf` makes it a *security* claim: "Version history keeps every earlier version — and those earlier versions still contain exactly what you have just removed." A per-**document** cap also does not bound the per-**principal** cost it was invented for: fifty versions each of unlimited documents is still unlimited. Meanwhile the thing that does bound it was missing on **two** write paths, not one: the quota is read before the write on upload, image assets, conversion sources and claim, and on neither `_save_new_version` nor `_create_document_from_bytes` — both bumped the counter and never looked at it, so an over-quota principal kept minting versions *and*, via `split` on the same endpoint, whole documents by the thousand. `core.limits.enforce_storage` is that check, on both; `test_version_quota.py` covers each. It uses a number already published and already on the usage panel, and — unlike a pruned history — the user can clear it by deleting something. | **Yes — §14 amended, §15's `version_retention_gc` removed, §19 and the tier table updated.** |
| 2026-08-02 | **`GET /api/documents/{id}/versions/` is paginated.** | It answered with every version in one bare array, and the workspace fetches it on every document open and again after each operation — 1.4 MB at 5 000 versions, uncompressed, for a panel that is not the default tab. The client never needed the whole history; it renders a scrolling list. The panel now says when it is showing a window, because a truncated list that looks complete is how somebody concludes their old versions were deleted. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **Job *inputs* are dropped at 30 days; job *rows* at 365.** | Two different things with two different reasons. The inputs — redaction patterns, replacement text, page lists — are a record of what was in somebody's document, and nothing user-visible depends on them (Settings renders type, status and time); that is `redaction_previews_purge`'s one-hour reasoning applied to every type at a month. The row is *our* record of an operation and the user can see it, so deleting it is observable and belongs in the privacy policy — at a window where nobody can actually observe it. The premise of the queue row that asked for this does not hold **at the scale it was measured at**, and the row said otherwise: it projected 220 buffers at 200k rows, and what was actually measured was a development table of a few thousand rows — a few hundred kilobytes, seven buffers. That does not refute a projection about 200k rows; it says only that we are nowhere near it, so the operational urgency was invented. What survives is the unbounded growth itself, which is why the fix is a window rather than nothing — and why the window is a year rather than the month an operational emergency would have justified. | **Yes — §15 gains both tasks.** |
| 2026-08-02 | **`jobs_purge` deletes `exports/{job_id}/` before the row, and refunds the bytes.** | `exports_purge` finds stale artefacts by iterating Job rows, so a deleted row makes the blob unreachable for ever — the same trap `guest_purge` and account deletion have each already had to write down. And once the row is gone nothing records that the quota charge was made, so the refund has to happen here. On a storage failure the row is left for tomorrow rather than deleting the blob's only pointer. | No (implements §13/§15) |
| 2026-08-02 | **The quota is enforced on both write paths, and `_save_export` is left open deliberately.** | Closing `_save_new_version` alone was the mistake an adversarial pass caught: `split` on a 2 000-page document lands in `_create_document_from_bytes` two thousand times from the *same* endpoint that had just started refusing `rotate_pages`, and per-page output duplicates shared resources, so a split→merge cycle grows an over-quota account every round. Both now check before the blob is written. `_save_export` is the one that stays charging-without-refusing: it is the escape hatch — how somebody over quota retrieves their work before deleting something — and it self-deletes in 24 h. Recorded because a claim that every door is shut is what stops the next reader checking. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **The history panel gets a "Show older versions" button.** | Paginating the versions endpoint without one fixed the *appearance* of a truncated history and removed access to the rest of it: revert is driven off that list, so v51 and older became unrevertible — falsifying the promise this change had just declined to prune history in order to keep. A window the user cannot open is a cap with extra steps. | No |
| 2026-08-02 | **`enforce_storage` reads the row, not the instance — and `split` is measured as a batch.** | `bump_storage` writes with `F()` and never refreshes the object it was handed, and a worker resolves its principal once per job. So inside the split loop every iteration read the value the *first* one saw: measured at `2898, 2898, 2898` while the row read `2898, 3769, 4639`, and a single request took an account from under quota to nearly twice it. The check was in the right place and read a number that could not move — decorative on exactly the path it was added for. It now costs one `SELECT` on a path that is about to write a blob. The batch check is the other half: a per-item refusal on item *k* leaves items 1..k-1 as real documents with real blobs and a real charge, attached to a job that says it failed, so `split` is measured whole before any of it is written. | No |
| 2026-08-22 | **"Am I in a browser" is asked of `isPlatformBrowser` **and** of the method about to be called.** | Node 25 defines a `localStorage` global that is a bare `{}` with no `getItem`, so `typeof localStorage === 'undefined'` — an *existence* check — handed three services a storage they could not use, taking 110 unit tests and all 29 prerendered routes down on node 25. Either check alone is insufficient and the spec says so: the platform check passes for node's half-storage under a browser-platform test, and a feature check alone would hand a prerender pass a storage nothing should ever write to. `core/browser-storage.ts` asks both. A storage that exists and then *throws* (Safari private mode) stays the callers' `try`/`catch` — this function is about what is reachable, not about what a reachable thing will do. | No (implementation detail) |
| 2026-08-22 | **The unit suite's node-25 storage repair is a `setupFiles` entry, deliberately separate from the product fix.** | Eight spec files drive `localStorage` *directly* — `clear()`, `setItem`, `getItem` — because what they assert is a real round-trip: a token written by another tab, an ad-consent choice surviving a reload. No product guard can help them, so `src/test-setup.ts` installs a memory `Storage` **only when the ambient one has no `getItem`**: node 24 and the containers are untouched. It cannot mask the defect it sits beside, because `browser-storage.spec.ts` builds its own half-present stub rather than trusting the host. | No |
| 2026-08-22 | **The page-drew markers are keyed to the URL that drew, not to a boolean.** | A boolean set at the first `pageRendered` keeps claiming "the page drew" through a save, a revert and a refresh-retry — the exact moments the viewer is showing nothing yet, and the exact false pass the assertion exists to prevent. Storing the URL makes the reset arithmetic: when `contentUrl()` changes the claim withdraws itself, with nothing to remember to clear. Same shape for the overlay raster, keyed on the object URL. | **Yes — `01-architecture.md` §18 rule 1.** |
| 2026-08-22 | ~~**The pixel sample walks rows at a 97 px stride and reads every pixel inside a sampled row.**~~ **Superseded the same night, in PR #24: the sample reads every pixel and stops counting at 64.** | *(The original rationale, kept because the correction is the interesting part: "97 is prime, so the walk cannot stay in step with the page's own rhythm … a grid coarse in both axes looks more rigorous and is not.")* Half of that is right and it is the half about **columns**. Rows are a different question, and on a page whose content is four short lines near the top, rows 0, 97, 194, 291 step over all four — measured on production, where the canvas read as **1 colour** while the screenshot showed the page and a full scan found **173, darkest at y = 141**. The e2e run passed on the same site minutes earlier because its canvas was a different height. `getImageData` copies the whole buffer either way, so the loop was never the expensive part: read every pixel, stop at 64, and only a blank page pays for the full scan. Also: pick the canvas with the most *on* it rather than the largest, since pdf.js keeps one same-sized canvas per page. | **Yes — §18 rule 1.** |
| 2026-08-22 | **The `version_conflict` race is fixed in the receiver, not in the six emitters.** | The mode components have emitted `output<Job>()` since Phase 3 and the job result already carries the new `seq`. What discarded it was `workspace.html`, which bound `(saved)="onEditSaved()"` with no `$event`, and six handlers that called a bare `reload()`. So the whole fix is `ViewerFacade.adopt(job)` plus six one-line handlers — nothing had to be re-plumbed to make the job available, only to stop throwing it away. `adopt` still reloads: the version list, outline and page count come from the server. It falls back to a plain reload when the result has no `seq`, because a `currentSeq` invented here would be the same defect with the blame reversed. | No (mechanism recorded in the queue row) |
| 2026-08-22 | **The skip guard asserts the skip *set*, and its own regression test is a shell check.** | A count would pass on four Gotenberg skips as readily as on the four Postgres-only query-plan tests, which is the failure it exists to catch — "1051 passed, 14 skipped" was read as green on 2026-08-21 with a tenth of the conversion surface untested. The guard's self-test runs the counter over two canned pytest transcripts before the real run: the thing under test is `test.sh`, so a pytest could not be the test for it.
