## Human review queue

Handoff programme (the nine CLI prompts from the 2026-08-21 status review) is tracked in `docs/reviews/handoffs/TRACKING.md`; PROGRESS stays canonical for phase status and evidence.

| Added | Item | Phase | GATE? | Resolved |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-24 | **Under `dir="rtl"` the whole app becomes horizontally scrollable by about 10 000 px, and it is not our markup.** Found by the phone workspace's RTL spot-check at 414 px: with `document.documentElement.dir = 'rtl'`, `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` reads **10 400** against a 414 px viewport and `scrollingElement.scrollLeft = -5000` sticks — the page really does scroll. Walking the DOM finds exactly one element outside the viewport: `<div aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true" class="sr-only" style="position:absolute; left:-10000px; width:1px; height:1px; overflow:hidden">`, appended to `<body>` — **`ngx-extended-pdf-viewer`'s own live region**, confirmed by locating the string in `node_modules/ngx-extended-pdf-viewer/fesm2022/ngx-extended-pdf-viewer.mjs`. A physical `left` is fine under LTR and is the whole defect under RTL, where it extends the scrollable area to the inline start. It appears only once the viewer has initialised, which is why `/` is clean and `/app/doc/:id` is not. The workspace's own layout mirrors correctly and adds no overflow — measured at 414 px in all nine modes, drawer open and closed, all **414**. Cheap defensive fix if it is ever wanted: one global rule neutralising a physical inset on that element; the honest fix is upstream. Only reachable by a locale the product does not yet ship, which is why it is a row and not a blocker — but it lands the day a Hebrew locale does. §8's "logical properties only" is our rule and this is a dependency breaking it inside our page. | 10 | No | ⬜ |
| 2026-08-24 | **Three number/select fields in workspace panels are 36 px on a phone because `!min-h-9` can only be answered by another `!important`.** Convert's DPI (`export-dpi`) and image format (`export-image-format`), and Compare's page offset (`compare-offset`), all carry Tailwind's important modifier in their templates. Below `md` those panels are bottom sheets, where §6's 36 px exemption — dense *desktop* toolbars — does not apply, and every other control in a sheet was raised to 44 px on 2026-08-24. These three were left because the only ways to reach them are an `!important` in the stylesheet, which is the same smell one level down, or removing `!min-h-9` from the templates, which would grow the fields by 8 px **on the desktop** and break the invariant that change was built on. The clean fix is to drop the important modifier and give the three fields a width-scoped size class instead; it is a template change with a desktop pixel-compare attached, and it is small. Recorded in design contract §3 (Phone workspace) so the exception is not silent. WCAG 2.1 AA is unaffected — 36 px clears SC 2.5.8's 24 px minimum; this is the contract's own stricter floor. | 12 | No | ⬜ |
| 2026-08-23 | **A refused operation says "Operation failed" and nothing else, including when the reason is a throttle the person only has to wait out.** Found by the by-eye pass on the version-cursor fix: pressing Redo into a spent throttle now correctly leaves the Undo/Redo chain exactly as it was, and the toast that explains it reads *"Operation failed"*. That is `Workspace.fail()` (`workspace.ts`), and the same generic line is what `convert.ts`, `edit.ts`, `forms.ts`, `protect.ts` and `sign.ts` raise from their own `fail()` on any HTTP error — the `next` branch has `job.error_message` to work with, the `error` branch has nothing but the status. Design contract §1 asks that "errors say what happened and what to do next", and a 429 has both to say. The one status worth naming is **429**: it is the only failure here that resolves by itself, the API already sends `Retry-After` (measured: 1–57 s depending on where in the window the request lands), and the person's correct action is to wait rather than to press again. `ThumbnailScheduler.isRefusal`/`refused` already parse exactly that header and could be the shared home for it. Deliberately **not** changed here: the workspace-debt prompt said to keep the existing toast, and rewording six panels' failure copy is a change with its own contract reading to make. | 12 | No | ⬜ |
| 2026-08-23 | **`phase-10-a11y`'s ceremony-keyboard spec can lose its session between `registerVerifiedAndLogin` and the upload that follows it, and nobody knows why.** Seen once in the first full `--e2e` run of `fix/workspace-debt-2026-08` (65 passed, this one failed); **passed in isolation seconds later in 7.8 s, and again at the same position in a second full run**, so it is not the branch — the branch changes no auth, token or guard code. What the trace showed: `registerVerifiedAndLogin` completed (its own `toHaveURL(/\/app\/dashboard/)` and `[data-test=verified]` assertions both passed), and then `uploadFiles` timed out for 20 s on `[data-test=file-input]` with the page snapshot showing **`/auth/register`**. That is `accountGuard` (`core/guards/account.guard.ts:21`), which redirects there when `TokenService.isAuthenticated` is false — and `isAuthenticated` is `!!this.access`, a plain presence check with no expiry in it. So `zen_access` was **absent from `localStorage` at guard time**. It was not cleared by the interceptor's refresh-failure path, which navigates to `/auth/login`, not `/auth/register`. Mechanism unpinned; the trace was lost to the isolated re-run, which deletes `test-results/`. Worth: capturing `localStorage` and the console at the failure (a `page.on('console')` in `registerVerifiedAndLogin`), and making that helper assert the dashboard rather than `goto` and hope — it ends with a bare `page.goto('/app/dashboard')` and no assertion, so a lost session is reported by whatever the caller does next. Same family as the two rows above it: the environment, not the product, and the gate paying for it. | 10 | No | ⬜ |
| 2026-08-23 | **A signature burnt onto a `/Rotate 90` or `/Rotate 270` page comes out 180° from upright, and the envelope footer becomes an unreadable vertical ribbon that also breaks `/verify`'s envelope match.** Found by H1's by-eye pass (§6) on envelope `ZEN-N6EV78`, whose source document is the `Rotated scan` fixture; **pre-existing — that branch changed no product code**, and an unrotated control from the same code path is correct in every respect (`evidence/h1/rotated-page-defect.png` versus `final.pdf`). Two distinct faults, one shared cause — orientation is not handled where position is. **(1) The 180°.** `signatures.py:199 _counter_rotation()` returns `(360 − page.rotation) % 360`, and it should be `page.rotation % 360`. The two agree at 0° and 180° and differ by exactly 180° at 90° and 270°, which is why nothing caught it: measured directly by inserting the same image on synthetic `/Rotate 0/90/180/270` pages both ways — at 90° and 270°, `_counter_rotation`'s value renders upside down and `page.rotation % 360` renders upright. It reaches the finished document through **both** signing paths: `esign/tasks.py:59 _burn_fields` (the ceremony) and `signatures.py:280 self_sign` (self-sign). **(2) The footer.** `signatures.py:291 stamp_envelope_footer` de-rotates its rect with `_place_rect` — so the box lands at the displayed foot, correctly — and then calls `insert_textbox` with **no `rotate=` argument at all**, unlike its two neighbours. On a rotated page that wide-and-short strip is a tall narrow column in unrotated space, so the line is laid out down it two characters at a time: `get_text()` returns `'En\nvel\nope\nZE\nN-\nN6\nEV\n78 …'`, `seal.py:200`'s `\bZEN-[A-Z2-9]{6}\b` matches nothing, and **`/verify` reports `found_code: None, known: false` for an envelope it owns** — measured against the local stack, where that envelope *is* in the database. `fit_text` (`signatures.py:218`) has the same omission, so date, checkbox and text fields are mis-oriented on rotated pages too. Not fixed here because the H1 branch is evidence-only. Worth a rendered-pixel assertion at 90° and 270° rather than a round-trip, which is the exact trap the 2026-08-01 §8 decision already records for annotations. | 8 | No | ✔ **Resolved 2026-08-23** (`fix/rotated-page-orientation`) — and it was the smaller half. The sign error was real and is fixed once, in `geometry.content_rotation`, but the sweep it triggered found **13 confirmed sites across five modules**, including a worse one: `01-architecture.md` §8's per-API table wrongly exempted `insert_htmlbox` from needing de-rotation, so **page numbers, headers and Bates numbers landed mid-page and sideways** on any rotated document (measured at display (0.336, 0.698) for a bottom-centre stamp). Both existing guard tests were false greens — one compared unrotated coordinates against display dimensions, the other asserted a bounding box that is symmetric under the 180° error it was written to catch. The footer needed a fitting strategy as well as a rotation, or the fix would have deleted it outright on A5 and receipt-sized rotated pages. Already-sealed envelopes are recovered by a whitespace-tolerant fallback in `seal.find_envelope_code`, since re-stamping would break their seal. **35 new tests, 24 of them red on the unfixed engine**; §8 amended. See the session log **"2026-08-23 (later) — Rotated pages: one wrong sign, thirteen places"**. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Should an interactive form widget turn with the page, or read upright to the reader?** The one member of the rotated-page family left undecided by `fix/rotated-page-orientation`, and deliberately so: it is a product question, not a defect. `forms.py:584` and `:636` call `add_widget` with no rotation, so a field added to a `/Rotate 90` page renders turned with the page like every other site in that sweep — but a widget's rotation is `/MK /R` in the appearance characteristics dictionary, which is a *different* mechanism from the `rotate=`/`/Matrix` used everywhere else, and viewers honour it with less consistency. The argument for turning it: the user places the field on a display-space preview, exactly as they place a signature, and every other surface now reads upright. The argument against: a widget is interactive, its appearance is regenerated by the viewer on focus and on value change, and a counter-rotation we bake in can be discarded or double-applied by a reader that has its own opinion. Nobody has measured what Acrobat, Preview and pdf.js each do with `/MK /R` on a rotated page, and that measurement is the whole decision. Until then the behaviour is unchanged and consistent with itself. | 5 | No | ⬜ |
| 2026-08-22 | **`phase-12`'s nudge assertion meets an unsettled frame more often on `fix/e2e-gate-hardening` than on `main`, and nobody knows why.** `settledBox` (`phase-12.spec.ts:54`) polls `boundingBox()` until two readings agree, and asserted non-null on the reading — so a null came back as `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'x')` rather than as "poll again". **Measured before the helper was hardened: 3 red in 12 on the branch, 0 red in 20 on `main`**, always the same crash. Bisected without a verdict: `main` + only the page-overlay change was 1 in 12, and the branch *minus* the page-overlay change was also 1 in 12 — no single edit accounts for it. What it is **not**: the final DOM carries the outline at the right size in the right place, and sampling `[data-test=overlay-selection]` inside the page at animation-frame resolution across the whole copy → paste → right-click-delete → undo → select sequence found **0 absent frames and 0 zero-sized frames in 181 samples**. With the helper polling instead of asserting, **14 of 14 green**. The plausible-but-unproven mechanism is that `drewUrl.set()` in `onImageLoad` adds one change-detection pass per raster load, widening the window in which Playwright reads a box mid-layout — `aspect.set()` alone used to be a no-op notification when the value repeated. | 2026-08-22 | **The version-Undo/Redo cursor is committed before the revert succeeds, so a refused Undo silently drops the chain.** `workspace.ts:447–457 stepVersion()` sets `versionCursor` to `{ceiling, content: target, expected: seq + 1}` and *then* dispatches `revert(target)`. If that job fails or is refused — a 429 from the guest throttle, `locked`, `version_conflict` — `currentSeq` never reaches `expected`, the cursor is treated as dead on the next press, and Undo silently falls back to `currentSeq − 1` with Redo disabled. **Measured live on production during the 2026-08-21 status review:** at v5 (content v1, Redo offering v2) a Redo that drew a 429 left the bar reading *"Undo the last change — back to v4"* / *"Nothing to redo"* — the chain the user was walking is gone, and the next Undo would revert to v4, which is not where they were. The user is told nothing, because from the bar's point of view nothing failed. A throttled guest (5 metered ops/min, 40 requests/min) is exactly who hits this. Fix: commit the cursor in `trackReload` **on success**, and restore the previous cursor on failure. Owner: `docs/reviews/handoffs/handoff-to-cli-workspace-debt-batch.md` item 5. | 12 | No | ✔ **Resolved 2026-08-23** (`fix/workspace-debt-2026-08`) — the chain a press would produce is carried through `revert()` as an argument and committed in the success branch only, so a refused revert leaves Undo and Redo saying exactly what they said before it. `expected` now comes from the finished job's own `seq` rather than `seq + 1`, which is only true while a revert appends exactly one version. Three cases in `workspace-undo.spec.ts` read the bar the way a person does — both buttons' `title` and `disabled` — for a refused Undo, a failed Redo, and the chain picked back up afterwards; all three fail on the previous code. The six existing cases are unchanged. |

| 2026-08-04 | **`record_password_failure`'s incr-then-set fallback can lose a count.** Two concurrent misses on a cold bucket both fall to `cache.set(key, 1)`, so the second overwrites rather than adds. Pre-existing, but the ten-second buckets L5 introduced make cold buckets six times more frequent. A Lua `INCR`+`EXPIRE` or `cache.add` then `incr` closes it. | 3 | No | ✔ **Resolved 2026-08-23** (`fix/backend-debt-2026-08`) — `cache.add(key, 0, timeout)` then `cache.incr(key)`: SETNX + INCR, each atomic on its own, and `add` is a no-op when the key exists so the loser of the race increments the winner's seed rather than overwriting it. The TTL is set by `add` alone and never extended by `incr`, so a bucket still ages out of the sliding window on schedule instead of being kept alive by the attempts it counts. The Lua alternative was considered and refused: it is one round trip instead of two, and it is a Redis-only code path in a module that also has to work against the LocMem cache the hermetic suite uses. Three new tests in `test_race_and_window_polish.py` drive a cache stub whose `set` overwrites the way the real one does — two cold misses read **2**, not 1 — and a fourth asserts `set` is never reached at all. The four existing window tests are untouched. |
| 2026-08-04 | **M6 — DNS rebinding defeats the URL→PDF guard, and no amount of regex closes it.** `apps/core/urlguard.py` resolves the name, checks the addresses, and then hands Gotenberg the **name**; Chromium resolves it again a moment later and connects to whatever it gets. Layer 2 does not cover this and the comments that said it did are now corrected: `--chromium-deny-list` matches on the URL string, so `report.example.com` passes it no matter what that name currently points at. The real fixes are all changes to *who does the fetching*: resolve once and hand the fetcher a pinned IP (with `Host:` preserved); or validate every hop's peer address at the socket; or put the converter behind an egress allowlist so the private space is unreachable whatever it is called. Layer 1 and the (now complete) layer-2 range list still stop the naive attempts, and JavaScript is off in the container, so this is a deliberate, bounded acceptance rather than an unknown. **Do not attempt a resolver rewrite as a side change.** | 6/10 | No | ⬜ |

Proven not to be a false green by deleting the `select_for_update` and re-running: `At index 1 diff: 202 != 429` — both requests counted zero active jobs before either inserted one, and a guest with `max_concurrent_jobs=1` got two. Three things the test has to get right to be about the lock at all are written down where they happen: `django_db(transaction=True)` (or the second thread blocks for ever on an uncommitted row), `apply_async` patched to a no-op (or the job is dispatched to workers on another database, or under eager execution finishes inside the request and frees the slot), and `connections.close_all()` in every thread's `finally` — leaked connections are the measured cause of the 2026-08-22 Postgres-budget row. The fix is to take the new `seq` from the job result the save already returns instead of waiting for the refetch — `trackReload` has the job in hand, but the mode components (`edit`, `forms`, `convert`, `sign`, `annotate`) emit a bare `(saved)` and would each need to carry it. | 2026-08-21 | **The e2e suite can silently test stale backend code.** Celery workers do not reload on change, so a local stack left up across a backend deploy runs the old task code while `api` (Django autoreload) runs the new. That is exactly how `phase-2b:130` was misdiagnosed twice — once as a product defect, once as a Playwright quirk — when the workers were 10 days old. A false *pass* is the worse case and would be invisible. Cheapest fix: have `infra/test.sh --e2e` restart the workers before driving the browser, or have `up.sh` warn when a worker container predates the newest file in `backend/`. | 3 | No | ✔ **Resolved 2026-08-22** (`fix/e2e-gate-hardening`) — **both** halves the row suggested, because they answer different questions. `infra/test.sh --e2e` restarts `worker-default`, `worker-heavy`, `worker-render` and `beat` before Playwright, polls `/api/health/` until it reports `status: ok` **and** `checks.workers` (120 s, then a loud failure), and prints each container's `StartedAt` so a green run says out loud what code it tested. `infra/up.sh` **warns without restarting** — it is also how you get back to a stack somebody is working in, and killing an in-flight job to make a point is not an improvement. "The newest file under `backend/`" is asked of `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, not of a directory walk: celery beat rewrites `celerybeat-schedule-shm` in that directory every few seconds, so a walk makes the warning fire on **every single run**, which is how a warning stops being read. Proven both ways: touching a tracked backend file makes all four report stale; restarting them clears it. Asking only about `checks.workers` turned out to be too narrow and was widened the same day — see the Postgres connection-budget row above, where the workers were fine and the database was not. |

Four things the browser found that no test had: the column outgrowing the screen to **1348 px against 844** in the three 900 px-render modes (the sideways assertion stayed green because a scrollbar narrows both of its sides); the **More sheet shipping with no head**; touch targets sized for a desk, the worst of them the comment row's D8 actions at **16 px**; and a focus ring clipped by the sheet's own scroller.
