**2026-08-21 (night) — The phase-12 local gate, and the two things the cloud could not see.**

The handoff ran end to end: local gate, eight browser checks by hand in both themes, four commits on `feat/phase-12-usability`, PR #20, self-review, merge (`ec8a33e`), production smoke. The handoff is archived at `docs/archived/2026-08-21-phase-12-cli-handoff.md` with an Executed banner and the three things it did not predict.

**The gate, in the container, because the host cannot run it.** Node on this machine is 25.2.1, which ships a `localStorage` global that exists and has no `getItem`; `token.service.ts` decides "am I in a browser" with `typeof localStorage === 'undefined'`, so a host `ng test` fails **108 tests** and a host `npm run build` fails **every prerendered route** with `TypeError: e?.getItem is not a function`. Nothing is wrong in production or in a browser — Railway and the `web` image are on node 24, and `infra/test.sh` runs lint and unit tests inside the container anyway — so the gate was run there and the guard is a **queue row**, not a phase-12 fix. It becomes real the day node 26 goes LTS. Numbers: deny-list drift identical across 4 infra copies · `ruff` + `mypy` **All checks passed!** · pytest **1061 passed, 4 skipped** · `ng lint` clean · `ng test` **396 across 47 files** · **29 prerendered routes** · `verify:prerender` green · Playwright **63 passed, 0 failed** · `data-test` **0 removed, 30 added** *(recorded here as 27; corrected 2026-08-22 — see the Phase 12 criterion)*.

**`phase-2b:130` passed.** The handoff listed it as a known pre-existing failure. It is not one any more — it was the stale Celery worker root-caused earlier the same day, and on this run the workers came up on current code, so the gate tested the code it thought it was testing. *(Qualified 2026-08-22: the original sentence said `infra/up.sh` "rebuilds and restarts the workers", which overstates it. `up.sh` runs `docker compose up -d --build`; compose recreates a container only when its image or definition changed, so a pure backend code change on a volume-mounted dev image recreates nothing and the restart here was **incidental**. The hazard is therefore still open — it is the 2026-08-21 "e2e suite can silently test stale backend code" row, and the explicit restart lands in `test.sh` via the e2e-gate-hardening prompt.)* (`phase-1`'s smoke failed once, under CPU contention from a concurrent host build, and passed on the clean run.)

**The e2e spec failed all three of its own tests the first time it was ever executed — and all three were the spec's fault.** The handoff's instruction to treat a failure as a test problem first was exactly right, and the reason was the same in every case: the spec acted before Angular's zoneless change detection had rendered the frame the previous action caused. (a) The shortcuts test pressed `⌘/` in the same breath as opening the document, so the key was sent into a page with nothing yet listening. (b) The nudge test read `boundingBox()` immediately after clicking a comment row, capturing the *previous* mark's outline, and then compared it against a different mark's position ten arrow presses later. (c) The redaction test clicked the drawn area one frame after disarming the rect tool — and while that tool is armed the area is `pointer-events: none`, so the click landed on the page behind it and selected nothing, which reads **exactly** like the D-C regression the test exists to catch. Each was verified by hand in a real browser before a line of product code was touched; the fix is three waits on observable state (`aria-pressed`, a settled box, a control that only exists once the workspace is up).

**Two real defects in the phase's own code, found by reading the diff as if somebody else wrote it.** First: Protect, Sign and the sign request builder carry `aria-keyshortcuts="Control+Z Meta+Z"` on their Undo buttons, and **only Annotate, Edit and Forms had a handler** — so three surfaces announced a shortcut to a screen reader and bound it to nothing, which is the precise failure the plan's D4 says the one-table design prevents. Each gains a handler taking undo and redo only; `cancel`, `delete` and the nudges stay the overlay's, and neither Protect nor Sign takes ⌘S, because applying a redaction destroys content and applying a signature seals the file. Five new unit cases, two of them proving the binding stays out of a focused field. Second: `menuTargetId` was set in all six surfaces' `onContextTarget` and **read nowhere** — a leftover from the array-input design that D6 replaced with a resolver function. Removed.

**Measured, not eyeballed.** ⌘C ⌘V offset the copy by 12 × 17 px on a 600 px page. Every menu row `getBoundingClientRect().height === 44`. One ArrowRight moved a shape **1.5 px** — 0.25 % of 600 — Shift moved it **14 px**, and at the edge `selection.right === overlay.right === 988.5` with the live region reading *"At the edge of the page"* rather than silently doing nothing. The bar went v7 → shown v6 → shown v5 across two Undos, with Redo offering v7 then v6: **two Undos, two versions back**, which is D-D closed. At 390 px, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 390` in both themes.

**Production.** Eight services and two databases ● Online, `/api/health/` ok, and `main-ZV5ZVVU6.js` on the live site is the same hash `npm run build` produced here. Smoke on the deployed bundle: upload → draw → right-click → a five-entry menu at 44 px a row (no *Paste here*, correctly — the clipboard was empty and an empty list means no entry), `⌘/` → 15 rows. **Not one console message of any level.**

**2026-08-21 — Independent post-deploy verification. 26/28 green; two failures, both found by measuring.**

Full report: `docs/reviews/2026-08-21-post-deploy-verification.md`. The checklist was fixed before any evidence was gathered, and every claim in it is labelled Verified / Inferred / Unverified.

**Method.** The deployed site was driven through a **transparent reverse proxy** to `https://zenpdf.up.railway.app` — same bytes, same status, same headers, automatable — while MIME and cache assertions were made with direct `curl`, so they cannot be an artefact of the proxy. Identity proved rather than assumed: a local `npm run build` at `dacf623` reproduces production's `main-EGLRBRDO.js` and `styles-PJ5P6VJ3.css` **byte for byte**.

**The three deploy targets are closed on the live site.** `.mjs` → `text/javascript` and all three `.wasm` → `application/wasm`; API errors `no-store` and successes `private, no-store` **while the deliberately-cached page raster kept its `max-age=86400`**; and the viewer draws — a 1201×1699 canvas with 7 distinct sampled colours, not a status code. The returning visitor recovers in one 410 + one mint with no banner and no error screen.

**The stack, end to end against the production API.** 24/24 tool pages · 9/9 workspace modes · **all 14 annotation types written and read back out of the file after a reload**, plus `image_stamp` and crop · forms (`full_name = 'AUDIT-FORM-VALUE'` in the download) · text editing (`EDITED-BY-AUDIT-2026` in the download) · encryption (decrypts with the chosen password, refuses a wrong one) · rotate/duplicate/delete versioning · **guest self-sign flattened as a 600×200 image XObject with zero annotations** · compare · convert · verify · the new version-level Undo (disabled at v1; v2·4pages → v3·5pages). 68 page loads across two themes: zero console errors, zero broken assets. Security headers intact everywhere **including the new `.mjs`/`.wasm` locations** — where an `add_header` block could easily have dropped CSP on the only files that carry executable code.

**Finding 1 — the toolbar above the page overflowed a phone (fixed here).** At a true 390 px layout Annotate's `scrollWidth` is 609; eight of nine modes are clean. Seven panes share that toolbar row and only the organize one ever had `flex-wrap`. The row needs 609 px — **445 of it before Undo/Redo were added on 2026-08-20**, so it was already over a phone's width and this session made it plainly so. **The more useful half is why the earlier check passed:** under mobile emulation Chrome answers an overflow by widening its own layout viewport to 609 and drawing at ~64 %, so `scrollWidth <= innerWidth` compared 609 against 609. Measure against `visualViewport.width`, or with emulation off. All seven rows now wrap (wrapping, not scrolling — these rows end in Save); after: every mode reports 390.

**Finding 2 — "Organize pages" did not open the page grid (pre-existing, fixed here).** `tool-page.ts` maps each interactive tool to its mode and `organize` was the one entry with an empty object; and the workspace's query-param handler accepted seven modes with `organize` and `view` not among them, so even an explicit `?mode=organize` rendered the reading view — presumably why the map entry was left empty. Both halves fixed; a new spec asserts the landing mode for all eight interactive tools.

**Finding 3 — a correction: the `separate`-extraction bug does not reproduce in production.** Extracting "2, 4" from a 5-page file returns **two** documents (`audit — page 2.pdf`, `audit — page 4.pdf`); after "Do another one" the dropzone re-arms with the CTA enabled; and the radio selects both via its label and directly. The e2e failure is in the harness — Playwright's `check()` not taking on that native radio, after which one document was the correct answer to what was actually sent. **The queue row below is re-scoped to the test rather than the product.**

Smaller observations (in the report, none of them failures): the palette shows no active state while `image_stamp` is armed; "4 of 2 page(s) differ" reads oddly when the page counts differ; and a malformed PNG is correctly refused with "That image could not be read." — checked against the same library the server uses before recording it.

**Gate at the deployed commit:** ng lint clean · 250 unit tests · build + 29 prerender + verify:prerender · ruff + mypy clean (173 files) · backend 1029 passed with the failure set **byte-identical to the pre-patch baseline**. With the two fixes: **258 unit tests**, 0 `data-test` removed.

**Not tested, deliberately:** anything requiring an account (no account is created on a live service), multi-party signing (SMTP is off), and the e2e suite (needs the full local stack). The two fixes here are verified against a local build and are **not deployed** — production still overflows in Annotate on a phone and still opens the reading view for "Organize pages". *(Superseded: PR #19 merged 2026-08-21 14:49 +03:00 and auto-deployed; both fixes verified on the live site the same day — status review L9. `/organize-pdf` lands on the grid with 3 tiles and zero viewer elements, and all nine workspace modes report `scrollWidth === 390` at a true 390 px viewport with emulation off.)*

Neither is a defect in the deploy. **2026-08-21 — The UI audit patch landed. The gate the cloud session could not run, run.**

The six commits below applied to `00ccb52` with `git am --3way` exactly as promised — no conflicts, no hand-resolution — and the patch is archived at `docs/archived/2026-08-20-ui-audit.patch` with the prompt that drove it at `docs/archived/2026-08-20-ui-audit-prompt.md`.

**The production "before", measured rather than assumed.** All three defects were confirmed live before the push, which is what makes the after meaningful: `GET /api/config/` answered 200 with **no `Cache-Control` header at all** and `Vary: Accept, origin`; `GET /api/documents/<nonexistent>/` answered `404` with `"message":"An error occurred."` and again no `Cache-Control`; and both `pdf.worker-6.0.1169.min.mjs` and `viewer-6.0.1169.min.mjs` were served as `content-type: application/octet-stream` alongside `x-content-type-options: nosniff` — a module script Chrome is obliged to refuse.

**Gate, on the full local stack.** ruff clean · mypy **clean, 173 source files** · backend pytest **1061 passed, 4 skipped** — fully green including the 5 new caching tests, and notably *without* the OCR/Ghostscript/unpaper absences a sandbox shows · `ng lint` clean · **250 unit tests, 250 passed (38 files)** · build + **29 prerendered routes** + `verify:prerender` green. The first unit run showed 3 failures, all `Test timed out in 5000ms` with `environment 590s` across 38 files; each passed in isolation in under 1.5 s and two of the three are specs this patch never touches, so it was jsdom setup contention, not logic — a clean re-run was 250/250.

**e2e: 59 passed, 1 failed — and the failure is not ours.** This is the run the cloud session could not do. The new spec it wrote blind, *"a text box shows its words on the page, and undo takes them back"*, **passed on its first execution**. The one failure is `phase-2b:130` *"extract takes the pages the visitor typed, together or apart"*, and it was reproduced on **`00ccb52` itself**, before a line of this patch: on `main` the second run does not start at all (after "Do another one" the file rows are empty and the CTA stays disabled, with the "separate" radio still on "One PDF"), while on the branch it starts and returns one document instead of two. Pre-existing, recorded below, deliberately **not** bundled into this change — see the new queue row.

**Data-test parity: 0 removed, 9 added** (`annot-undo`, `annot-redo`, `back`, `free-text-hint`, `overlay-text`, `overlay-text-editor`, `undo-version`, `workspace-bar-minimal`, `workspace-throttled`), and routes, tool slugs, `index.html`, legal and e-sign copy are untouched by the diff.

**2026-08-20 — Full UI audit + production bug hunt (Cowork). Four fixes, none deployed yet.**

Owner's report: "most features do not work in production", and locally "edit features need to be reworked — in annotate, the text box is bold, doesn't show the text at all; no clear undo button for edits." Both were reproduced and both are fixed; the work is a `git format-patch` on `00ccb52` awaiting `git am` + push (see the handoff below).

**Method.** Production was driven in the owner's own Chrome, and then reproduced in the cloud sandbox by serving the real production bundle from a local server that copies Railway's MIME map, CSP and `nosniff` **and proxies `/api` to the live API** — a debuggable production. Playwright cannot reach the internet from the sandbox but it can reach localhost, so every finding below was reproduced, fixed and re-verified in a real browser against the real backend.

**1 — `GET /api/config/` answered `410` from the browser cache, for every token, for ever.** *(the headline defect)* Guest tokens expire after 24 h. The first request with a stale one answers `410 guest_expired`. That response carried **no `Cache-Control`**, so Chrome stored it heuristically, keyed on URL + `Vary` (`Accept, origin` — the credential is not in it). Every later `/api/config/` in that browser was then answered 410 *from cache*, with tokens minted seconds earlier: the client cleared each fresh session as fast as it created one, uploads landed in sessions nothing could read, and `/app/doc/<id>` showed "An error occurred." Proven by counting requests at the proxy — the server saw **one** 410; the browser replayed it five times. Fixed with `ApiCacheControlMiddleware`: an error is never stored, a success keeps whatever the view chose and otherwise gets `private, no-store`.

**2 — a late `410` wiped a live session.** Two `/api/config/` calls made with the same expired token both come back 410, and the second landed *after* a fresh session had been minted and a file uploaded into it. `onSessionExpired` now takes the credential the failing request carried and ignores a verdict about one it no longer holds. On top of that, `guest_expired` now mints a fresh session and **replays the request once**, so a returning visitor simply carries on; the banner is kept for the case that really lost something.

**3 — the workspace viewer never loaded in production, and nobody could see why.** Two independent blockers, both invisible locally. (a) ngx-extended-pdf-viewer feature-detects the browser with an **inline `<script>`**, which `script-src 'self'` refuses — so the viewer never initialised and `…/content/` was never requested at all (the open defect recorded 2026-08-10). `[useInlineScripts]="false"` takes the library's own external `op-chaining-support.js` instead. (b) nginx served pdf.js's `.mjs` modules as `application/octet-stream` — stock `mime.types` knows neither `.mjs` nor `.wasm` — which `nosniff` (rightly) makes fatal for a module script. Both nginx configs pin the type. **Verified:** `viewer-*.min.mjs`, `pdf.worker-*.min.mjs` and `/api/documents/{id}/content/` all 200, page drawn, zero console errors.

**4 — the text box, and undo.** A `free_text` annotation drew an empty rectangle with a 2 pt frame in the highlighter's yellow and put its words in a 10 px badge floating above the box, truncated at 24 characters, editable only in the right sidebar — exactly "bold, doesn't show the text at all". Text is now drawn inside its own rect at its own point size and colour (converted against the page's real width in points), edited in place with the caret already in it, committed on blur. Colour and opacity are held per tool family so text is ink, not highlighter. And Undo/Redo (⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z) in the annotate bar over a snapshot-per-gesture history, plus a version-level **Undo** in the workspace bar — the revert that was previously three clicks deep in the History tab. **Verified end to end:** typed, saved, downloaded; the file carries `/FreeText` with the text at `0.2 0.176 0.141 rg /Helv 12`.

**Also found and fixed.** The workspace never filled the screen (host was not declaring itself a flex item — a 900 px screen gave a 318 px reading pane in every mode). It was unusable on a phone: 208 px rail + page + 256 px rail overflowed 390 px, the page scrolled sideways and the mode nav sat off the right edge — panes stack below `md` now and the bar scrolls inside itself. The loading and error screens had no header at all. A `429` was dressed as a fatal error; it now says what it is and holds the retry for the seconds the server named. `Http404` reached the client as the literal words "An error occurred." File sizes were rendered only in megabytes, so a 40 KB page read "0.0 MB". And `/merge-pdf` sent `document_ids` in network order, against its own promise — the known-not-fixed item from 2026-08-19.

**Swept, working.** All 24 tool pages run end to end against the live API (upload → job → result/workspace), light and dark, zero console errors, zero 4xx; landing, legal, verify, auth, dashboard and 404 all render with an h1 and no errors; RTL mirrors correctly including Hebrew in a text box; `data-test` parity 0 removed / 9 added.

**Gate.** `ng lint` clean · **250 unit tests** (18 new) · build + 29 prerendered routes + `verify:prerender` green · ruff + mypy clean (173 files) · backend suite diffed against baseline — **no new failures** (the sandbox's usual OCR / Ghostscript / unpaper / admin-allowlist absences are unchanged), 5 new backend tests. e2e spec extended for the text box; **e2e not run** — it needs the full local stack, which the sandbox cannot host.

**➡ Handoff.** Six commits (five fixes + this record) exist only as **`.zen-ui-audit-2026-08-20.patch` in the repo root on the Mac** (the sandbox cannot push to `yhaspel/ZenPDF`); the prompt that drives it is committed at `docs/archived/2026-08-20-ui-audit-prompt.md` (executed 2026-08-21 — see the entry above). Apply with `git am`, re-run the gate locally, push — the push is the deploy. No migrations, no new npm dependencies, no env changes. **The nginx change means the `web` service must rebuild**; `infra/railway/**` is in its watch patterns, so a push does it.

**2026-08-19 — The page-tools fix shipped to production (PR #14, #15)**

- **Landed:** the 2026-08-18 patch below, applied verbatim, plus the plan itself archived at `docs/archived/2026-08-18-extract-delete-page-selection.patch`. Local gate on the full stack: ruff + mypy clean (172 source files), `apps/pdf_engine` + `apps/documents` **689 passed**, `ng lint` clean, **232 unit tests**, build + **29 prerendered routes** + `verify:prerender` green. Two pytest failures (`test_an_html_file_becomes_a_pdf`, `test_ten_pages_finish_inside_the_budget`) were a Gotenberg timeout and an OCR wall-clock budget losing the race to a concurrent production build; both pass in isolation and neither touches the changed code.
- **Found by the review pass and fixed (cf7dc29):** `text-ink` on the two `.cfield` choice rows never applied. Tailwind utilities compile inside `@layer utilities`; `label { color: var(--color-ink-muted) }` in `styles.scss` is **unlayered**, and an unlayered author rule beats every cascade layer regardless of specificity. The option lines therefore painted `--color-ink-muted` against `--color-ink-faint` sub-lines — 1.26:1 apart in light, 1.50:1 in dark, where the §3 choice-row spec written by the same change calls for a `--color-ink` line. `!text-ink` (its four neighbours already carried `!` for this reason) restores 2.41:1 / 2.58:1. **The same latent instance is at `features/sign/ceremony.html:69`**, where the pattern was copied from — pre-existing, untouched.
- **§10 registry amended (PR #15):** the `extract_pages` row gained `separate` and the note that it returns `{documents: [...]}` rather than a version.
- **Production verification** (Chrome, `https://zenpdf.up.railway.app`, all six Railway services green): extract of "4, 1" from a 5-page fixture dispatched `pages: [3, 0]` and returned a 2-page PDF reading **PAGE 4, PAGE 1** — typed order preserved end to end. Separate mode returned `zen5 — page 5.pdf` + `zen5 — page 2.pdf`. Delete of "4, 2, 4" dispatched `pages: [1, 3]` (deduped, ascending) and left **PAGE 1, PAGE 3, PAGE 5**. Both refusals fire with the real count ("This PDF has 5 pages, so there is no page 9"; "That would delete every page…"), a corrected selection reused the upload (one `POST /api/documents/` across two attempts), zero console errors, no login prompt anywhere, and both themes correct in production — `color-scheme` resolves per theme and the choice rows read at `--color-ink`.
- **Known, not fixed — `tool-page.ts` `uploadAll` orders by response, not by pick.** `uploaded.push(body)` inside each concurrent upload's `next` means `/merge-pdf` sends `document_ids` in network order, and `tasks.py` merges strictly positionally — add a 20 MB chapter then a 200 KB cover and the cover lands second, non-deterministically, against `tool-pages.ts`'s promise that "Page order follows the order you add the files." `/compare` shares the race (the diff direction can invert). Pre-existing and a different tool, so it was left out of #14 rather than folded in; the fix is the same "slot, not push" the same file now uses in `onSuccess`, and it wants its own change with a test.

**2026-08-18 — Defect fix: the page tools acted on page 1 only (Cowork session, owner-reported)**

- **What was wrong:** `/extract-pdf-pages` and `/delete-pdf-pages` dispatched `pages: [0]` hardcoded, so "Extract PDF pages" always extracted page one and "Delete pages" always deleted page one — plural promises that could act on a single page. The copy sent people to the workspace to choose pages, which is not what a tool page is for (§21.6: the page *is* the tool).
- **Fix (frontend):** new options row on both tool pages — a page field taking numbers and ranges in any order (`1, 3, 5-8`), parsed by `core/page-spec.ts` (1-based on screen, 0-based over the wire per §8), with the CTA disabled until the selection parses. Extract adds a choice of result: one PDF holding the selection (typed order preserved — a selection can reorder as it extracts) or a separate PDF per page. Two refusals never reach the engine: a page the document does not have (answered with the real page count, learned at upload) and a deletion that would empty the document. A corrected selection reuses the already-uploaded document instead of uploading the file twice (dropped again on any operation failure, so an expired guest session self-heals); result rows land in page order, not network order (fixes the same race for split's parts); the page field is `dir="ltr"` so a future RTL locale reads "1, 3, 5-8" as typed.
- **Fix (backend):** `extract_pages` gained `separate` (schema + `engine/pages.py: extract_pages_each` + `documents/tasks.py`), producing one document per page titled `<source> — page N` (duplicates collapse). `separate` implies `as_new_document`.
- **Design contract amended** (gap rule): §3 gains the **choice-row** pattern (`.cfield` rows wrapping native radios — `.seg` is 36 px and this is a marketing surface with a 44 px floor) and §4's tool-page entry gains the **options row**. Also fixed a theming hole found while checking dark mode: `color-scheme` was never declared, so every native control — the new radios, the existing form-field radios, checkboxes, scrollbars — rendered from the light palette in dark mode (an unchecked radio came out a solid white disc). Now `:root { color-scheme: light }` / `.dark { color-scheme: dark }`, noted in §5.
- **Verification:** frontend **232 unit tests** pass (17 new: 9 parser, 8 widget — dispatch params, both refusals, disabled CTA, upload reuse); backend `apps/pdf_engine` + `apps/documents` **676 passed** (4 new; the 5 failures are the sandbox's missing OCR/Ghostscript binaries, unrelated); ruff + mypy + `ng lint` clean; production build + 29 prerendered routes + `verify:prerender` green; both tool pages screenshot-verified in light/dark at 1280 and 390 px, plus RTL and the invalid-input state. e2e coverage added to `phase-2b.spec.ts` (not run — needs the full local stack).

- Owner questioned the login gate. Outcome: **access model changed to anonymous-first**; paid tier stays out of v1 but limits are now tier-resolved so billing is addable without a refactor. Full rationale in the Decisions log above.
- **Plan docs amended (no application code touched):** `01-architecture.md` §§1, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 13, 15, 16, 17, 19, 20 + **new normative §21 (Access model)**; **new** `phase-02b-anonymous-access.md`; `README.md` (locked decisions, index, dependency graph, ordering); `02-feature-matrix.md` (access-tier section + platform rows); `phase-00` + `phase-01` (retrofit banners + superseded criteria), `phase-03`–`phase-07` (guest-parity + tool-page acceptance criteria; phase-03 guest annotation authorship), `phase-08` (self-sign guest / requests account-only), `phase-09` (rescoped — abuse controls and tool pages moved out; throttle matrix defers to §16), `phase-10` (isolation sweep covers guests), `prompt-2-phases-03-07.md` (stale-precondition banner — **do not run it until 2B lands**).
- **Adversarial review pass run over the amended plan** (independent agent, read against the *built* code). 18 findings, all folded in — the load-bearing ones: the worker layer resolves ownership through `job.user` not `request.user` (so the grep-test gate had to widen, and `owner=None` would both violate the new constraint and match any guest's docs); storage accounting is `User`-only in `services.py`/`tasks.py`/`views.py`; **`MAX_PAGES` is not enforced anywhere today** despite §17 claiming it is; claim had to cover jobs + usage counters, not just documents; "heavy op" meant three different things and the collision would have put a CAPTCHA in front of `/merge-pdf`; the viewer's `httpHeaders` and `pdf-thumbnail.ts` bypass the HTTP interceptor entirely; and the absolute "no guest-triggerable email" claim was false (Phase 8 mails on ceremony actions) — narrowed to *address selection*.
- **Status unchanged for Phases 0–2** — they remain ✅; 2B retrofits them rather than reopening them. Their acceptance criteria must still pass for authenticated users after the refactor (2B acceptance criterion 10).
